, and needs to satisfy certain assumptions to form a cusp at the distance of order
n\overline{t}
.

Assumption 5.1
.

We assume that
\rho_{0}=0
on
[E_{-},E_{+}]
, for some
E_{-}<E_{+}
with
E_{+}-E_{-}\asymp\overline{t}^{3/2}
.
Let
\{\gamma_{i}\}_{i\in\llbracket-M,N\rrbracket}
be the scale
n^{-1}
quantiles starting from
E_{-}
and
E_{+}
. Namely, we let
\gamma_{0}=E_{+}
; for
i\in\llbracket 1,N\rrbracket
or
i\in\llbracket 1,M\rrbracket
, let
\gamma_{i}
or
\gamma_{-i}
satisfy that,

\int_{E_{+}}^{\gamma_{i}}\rho_{0}(x){\rm d}x=i/n,\quad\text{or}\quad\int_{\gamma_{-i}}^{E_{-}}\rho_{0}(x){\rm d}x=i/n.

We assume that
\rho_{0}=0
on
(-\infty,\gamma_{-M})\cup(\gamma_{N},\infty)
.
In addition, we assume that for
i\in\mathbb{N}
, when
i\lesssim\overline{t}^{2}n
,

\gamma_{i}-E_{+}\asymp\overline{t}^{1/6}(i/n)^{2/3},\quad E_{-}-\gamma_{-i}\asymp\overline{t}^{1/6}(i/n)^{2/3};

and when
i\gtrsim\overline{t}^{2}n
,
i\leq N
or
M
,

\gamma_{i}-E_{+}\asymp(i/n)^{3/4},\quad E_{-}-\gamma_{-i}\asymp(i/n)^{3/4}.

For
\{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}\}_{i\in[\![{-M,N}]\!]}
, we assume that it is approximated by
\rho_{0}
, in the following sense:

•

{\mathsf{d}}_{0}-E_{+}n\lesssim n^{1/3+\epsilon_{4}}\overline{t}^{1/6}
and
{\mathsf{d}}_{-1}-E_{-}n\lesssim n^{1/3+\epsilon_{4}}\overline{t}^{1/6}
;

•

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

(5.1)

|\{i\in\llbracket-M,N\rrbracket:{\mathsf{d}}_{i}<xn\}|-|\{i\in\llbracket-M,N\rrbracket:\gamma_{i}<x\}|\lesssim n^{\epsilon_{4}}.

5.1.
Cusp location

We now determine the cusp location of NBRW under Assumption
5.1
, in a way indicated by Lemma
7.6
.
For this purpose, we consider the Stieltjes transform

m_{0}(z)=\int\frac{\rho_{0}(x)}{z-x}{\rm d}x
.
Below are some basic properties of
m_{0}
for
z\in(E_{-},E_{+})
, which are straightforward to check.

(1)

m_{0}^{\prime}(z)=-\int\frac{\rho_{0}(x)}{(z-x)^{2}}{\rm d}x<0
for any
z\in(E_{-},E_{+})
, and

(5.2)

|m_{0}^{\prime}(z)|=\int\frac{\rho_{0}(x)}{(z-x)^{2}}{\rm d}x\asymp\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\frac{1}{(z-\gamma_{i})^{2}}\asymp\overline{t}^{-1/4}((z-E_{-})\wedge(E_{+}-z))^{-1/2},

when
z\in(E_{-}+n^{-2/3}\overline{t}^{1/6},E_{+}-n^{-2/3}\overline{t}^{1/6})
.

(2)

When
z\in(E_{-}+n^{-2/3}\overline{t}^{1/6},E_{+}-n^{-2/3}\overline{t}^{1/6})
, we have

(5.3)

|m_{0}(z)|\leq\int\frac{\rho_{0}(x)}{|z-x|}{\rm d}x\asymp\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\frac{1}{|z-\gamma_{i}|}\lesssim 1.

(3)

With
m_{0}^{\prime\prime}(z)=\int\frac{2\rho_{0}(x)}{(z-x)^{3}}{\rm d}x
, we can deduce that
m_{0}^{\prime\prime}(E_{-}+n^{-2/3}\overline{t}^{1/6})>0
,
m_{0}^{\prime\prime}(E_{+}-n^{-2/3}\overline{t}^{1/6})<0
, and

(5.4)

m_{0}^{\prime\prime}(z)\asymp\overline{t}^{-1/4}((z-E_{-})\wedge(E_{+}-z))^{-3/2},

for any
z\in(E_{-},E_{+})
with
(z-E_{-})\wedge(E_{+}-z)<c\overline{t}^{3/2}
for a small enough constant
c>0
.

(4)

m_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z)=-\int\frac{6\rho_{0}(x)}{(z-x)^{4}}{\rm d}x<0
for any
z\in(E_{-},E_{+})
, so
m_{0}^{\prime\prime}
is decreasing in
(E_{-},E_{+})
.
Moreover,

(5.5)

m_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z)=-\int\frac{6\rho_{0}(x)}{(z-x)^{4}}{\rm d}x\asymp\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\frac{1}{(z-\gamma_{i})^{4}}\asymp\overline{t}^{-4},

whenever
(z-E_{-})\wedge(E_{+}-z)\asymp\overline{t}^{3/2}
.

(5)

For any
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z\in(E_{-},E_{+})
and
(\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z-E_{-})\wedge(E_{+}-\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z)\asymp\overline{t}^{3/2}
, we have

(5.6)

m_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z)=\int\frac{24\rho_{0}(x)}{(z-x)^{5}}{\rm d}x\lesssim\overline{t}^{-11/2}.

We further denote (see
7.7
below)

f_{0}(z)=e^{m_{0}(z)}\frac{\beta}{1-\beta}
.
We then find the cusp location, using formulas inspired by the complex slope (see
7.6
below).

Lemma 5.2
.

There exist
x_{c}\in\mathbb{R}
,
t_{c}>0
, and
z_{c}\in(E_{-},E_{+})
, such that

(5.7)

x_{c}=z_{c}+t_{c}\frac{f_{0}(z_{c})}{f_{0}(z_{c})+1},

(5.8)

1+t_{c}\frac{f_{0}^{\prime}(z_{c})}{(f_{0}(z_{c})+1)^{2}}=0,

(5.9)

f_{0}^{\prime\prime}(z_{c})-\frac{2f_{0}^{\prime}(z_{c})^{2}}{f_{0}(z_{c})+1}=0.

In addition, we have

(5.10)

t_{c}\asymp\overline{t},\quad(E_{+}-z_{c})\wedge(z_{c}-E_{-})\asymp\overline{t}^{3/2},\quad-t_{c}<z_{c}-x_{c}<0,\quad x_{c}-z_{c},\,t_{c}+z_{c}-x_{c}\asymp t_{c}.

The cusp should be present around the location
(nx_{c},nt_{c})
.
The strategy to prove this lemma is to first determine
z_{c}
using (
5.9
), then
t_{c}
using (
5.8
), and finally
x_{c}
using (
5.7
).

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

Note that (
5.9
) is equivalent to
g(z_{c})=0
, where for
z\in(E_{-},E_{+})
,
g(z)
is defined as

g(z):=[(m_{0}^{\prime}(z))^{2}+m_{0}^{\prime\prime}(z)]\beta(\beta+(1-\beta)e^{-m_{0}(z)})-2(m_{0}^{\prime}(z))^{2}\beta^{2}.

Using the above basic properties (
5.2
), (
5.3
), and (
5.4
), we get that
g(E_{-}+n^{-2/3}\overline{t}^{1/6})>0
and
g(E_{+}-n^{-2/3}\overline{t}^{1/6})<0
.
Thus, we can find
z_{c}\in(E_{-}+n^{-2/3}\overline{t}^{1/6},E_{+}-n^{-2/3}\overline{t}^{1/6})
such that
g(z_{c})=0
.
For such
z_{c}
, (
5.2
) and
g(z_{c})=0
imply that

m_{0}^{\prime\prime}(z_{c})\lesssim(m_{0}^{\prime}(z_{c}))^{2}\lesssim\overline{t}^{-1/2}((z_{c}-E_{-})\wedge(E_{+}-z_{c}))^{-1}.

Then by (
5.4
), we must have that
(z_{c}-E_{-})\wedge(E_{+}-z_{c})\asymp\overline{t}^{3/2}
.
So by (
5.2
), we have
m_{0}^{\prime}(z_{c})\asymp\overline{t}^{-1}
, and by (
5.3
), we have
m_{0}(z_{c})\lesssim 1
.
The number
t_{c}
is then determined by (
5.8
), which yields that

t_{c}=-\frac{(e^{m_{0}(z_{c})}\beta+(1-\beta))^{2}}{m_{0}^{\prime}(z_{c})\beta(1-\beta)e^{m_{0}(z_{c})}}\asymp\overline{t}.

Finally,
x_{c}
is solved from (
5.7
).
In particular, (
5.7
) and the fact that
m_{0}(z_{c})\lesssim 1
imply that
-t_{c}<z_{c}-x_{c}<0
and
x_{c}-z_{c},\,t_{c}+z_{c}-x_{c}\asymp t_{c}
.
∎

5.2.
Kernel approximation

As discussed before, for the NBRW
{\mathsf{A}}
, the set
\{({\mathsf{a}}_{i}({\mathsf{t}}),{\mathsf{t}})\}_{i\in\llbracket-M,N\rrbracket,{\mathsf{t}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
is a determinantal point process on
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
The kernel is given in
[
GP19
, Theorem 2.1]
as

(5.11)

K^{\textnormal{Bernoulli}}({\mathsf{t}}_{1},{\mathsf{x}}_{1};{\mathsf{t}}_{2},{\mathsf{x}}_{2})=\mathds{1}_{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}\geq{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}\mathds{1}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}>{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}(-1)^{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1}\binom{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}\\
+\frac{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}!}{({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1)!}\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}}\int\limits_{{\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}-\mathbf{i}\infty}^{{\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}+\mathbf{i}\infty}{\rm d}{\mathsf{z}}\oint\limits_{\textnormal{all ${\mathsf{w}}$ poles}}{\rm d}{\mathsf{w}}\,\frac{({\mathsf{z}}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1)_{{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1}}{({\mathsf{w}}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1})_{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}+1}}\frac{1}{{\mathsf{w}}-{\mathsf{z}}}\frac{\sin(\pi{\mathsf{w}})}{\sin(\pi{\mathsf{z}})}\left(\frac{1-\beta}{\beta}\right)^{{\mathsf{w}}-{\mathsf{z}}}\prod_{i=-M}^{N}\frac{{\mathsf{z}}-{\mathsf{d}}_{i}}{{\mathsf{w}}-{\mathsf{d}}_{i}},

for any
{\mathsf{x}}_{1},{\mathsf{x}}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
{\mathsf{t}}_{1},{\mathsf{t}}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.
Here, we recall the Pochhammer symbols and the binomial coefficients defined at the beginning of
Section

2
.
The integration contours for
{\mathsf{z}}
and
{\mathsf{w}}
are as follows: the
{\mathsf{z}}
contour is the straight vertical line
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}({\mathsf{z}})={\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}
traversed upwards, and the
{\mathsf{w}}
contour is a positively (counter-clockwise) oriented circle or a union of two circles encircling all the
{\mathsf{w}}
poles
\{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1},{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1}+1,\ldots,{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-1,{\mathsf{x}}_{1}\}\cap\{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}\}_{i\in[\![{-M,N}]\!]}
of the integrand, except the pole at
{\mathsf{w}}={\mathsf{z}}
.

With proper scaling, this kernel
K^{\textnormal{Bernoulli}}
should be approximated by the Pearcey kernel
K^{\textnormal{Bernoulli}}
, given by (
2.11
). This is the main task of this section.
For this purpose, we denote

(5.12)

\begin{split}A&=t_{c}^{4}\int\frac{\rho_{0}(x)}{4(z_{c}-x)^{4}}{\rm d}x-\frac{t_{c}^{4}}{12(t_{c}+z_{c}-x_{c})^{3}}-\frac{t_{c}^{4}}{12(x_{c}-z_{c})^{3}},\quad B=(x_{c}-z_{c})t_{c}^{-1}.\end{split}

By (
5.10
), we have
B>0
and
B\asymp 1
.
We also have
A>0
and
A\asymp 1
, which will be proved later as Lemma
5.6
.
Then,
2.9
is an immediate consequence of the next proposition.

Proposition 5.3
.

Suppose Assumption
5.1
holds. Take
x_{c}\in\mathbb{R}
and
t_{c}>0
from Lemma
5.2
, and define
A,B
as in (
5.12
).
Take any
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,
{\mathsf{x}}_{1},{\mathsf{x}}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
{\mathsf{t}}_{1},{\mathsf{t}}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
such that
|\tau_{1}|,|\tau_{2}|,|\gamma_{1}|,|\gamma_{2}|\lesssim 1
, and
{\mathsf{t}}_{1}=nt_{c}+n^{1/2}\tau_{1}+\OO(1)
,
{\mathsf{t}}_{2}=nt_{c}+n^{1/2}\tau_{2}+\OO(1)
,
{\mathsf{x}}_{1}=nx_{c}+Bn^{1/2}\tau_{1}+n^{1/4}\gamma_{1}+\OO(1)
,
{\mathsf{x}}_{2}=nx_{c}+Bn^{1/2}\tau_{2}+n^{1/4}\gamma_{2}+\OO(1)
.
Then, we have

\Big|(-1)^{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}B^{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}(1-B)^{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+{\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}K^{\textnormal{Bernoulli}}({\mathsf{t}}_{1},{\mathsf{x}}_{1};{\mathsf{t}}_{2},{\mathsf{x}}_{2})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}n^{1/4}A^{1/4}B(1-B)}\\
\times K^{\textnormal{Pearcey}}\Big(\frac{-\tau_{1}}{2A^{1/2}B(1-B)},\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\sqrt{2}A^{1/4}B(1-B)};\frac{-\tau_{2}}{2A^{1/2}B(1-B)},\frac{\gamma_{2}}{\sqrt{2}A^{1/4}B(1-B)}\Big)\Big|\lesssim n^{-1/4-\epsilon_{3}},

if
\tau_{1}=\tau_{2}
and
{\mathsf{t}}_{1}={\mathsf{t}}_{2}
, or
|\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}|>n^{-\epsilon_{3}}
.

The rest of this section is devoted to proving this proposition.
Without loss of generality, below we assume that
x_{c}=0
, by shifting
\rho_{0}
and
\{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}\}_{i\in[\![{-M,N}]\!]}
.
Then by (
5.10
) and Lemma
5.6
below,
we have
-t_{c}<E_{-}<z_{c}<E_{+}<0
and
E_{+},E_{-}+t_{c}\asymp t_{c}
, and
E_{+}-z_{c},z_{c}-E_{-}\asymp\overline{t}^{3/2}
.
Therefore, we have (see Figure
5
)

(5.13)

({\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1}+1)/n,\,({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+1)/n<{\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n<z_{c}<{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n<({\mathsf{x}}_{1}-1)/n,({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-1)/n.

z_{c}
E_{-}
E_{+}
{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n
{\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n
-t_{c}
0
{\mathsf{x}}_{1}/n
({\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1})/n

Figure 5
.
An illustration of the initial configuration
\{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}\}_{i\in[\![{-M,N}]\!]}
(blue), and the locations of
E_{-}
,
E_{+}
,
z_{c}
,
-t_{c}
,
0
, and
({\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1})/n
,
{\mathsf{x}}_{1}/n
.

Our main task is now to analyze the contour integral in (
5.11
).
By separating the terms containing
{\mathsf{z}}
or
{\mathsf{w}}
, we need to study the integral of
\mathsf{f}({\mathsf{z}},{\mathsf{w}})=\frac{1}{{\mathsf{w}}-{\mathsf{z}}}\exp(n\mathsf{D}_{2}({\mathsf{z}}/n)-n\mathsf{D}_{1}({\mathsf{w}}/n))
, with the same contours.
Here,
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}
are two
key functions
to be defined in Section
5.3
.
These functions have three critical points near
z_{c}
. From that, we will show that
\mathsf{D}_{1}(z)-\mathsf{D}_{1}(z_{c})
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)-\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c})
are approximately
-t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{4}
around
z_{c}
.
In light of this, we then use the steepest descent method as follows: we deform the contours of
{\mathsf{z}}
and
{\mathsf{w}}
, so that
{\mathsf{z}}/n
passes through
z_{c}
vertically, and
{\mathsf{w}}/n
passes through
z_{c}
in the
e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/4}
,
e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/4}
,
e^{5\pi\mathbf{i}/4}
,
e^{7\pi\mathbf{i}/4}
directions, and these contours roughly follow the steepest descent curves of
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(\mathsf{D}_{2})
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(\mathsf{D}_{1})
away from
z_{c}
.
Then, for the integral of
\mathsf{f}({\mathsf{z}},{\mathsf{w}})
, the main contribution comes from the part of the contours where
|{\mathsf{z}}/n-z_{c}|
and
|{\mathsf{w}}/n-z_{c}|
are of order
\OO(t_{c}n^{-1/4})
. We will later call this part the ‘inner part’, and the remaining part the ‘outer part’.
We will do a careful asymptotic analysis of the inner part to obtain the Pearcey kernel (
2.11
), and show that (under appropriate scaling) the outer part decays to zero as
n\to\infty
.

As already mentioned in the introduction, the steepest descent method has been extensively used to do asymptotic analysis for determinantal point processes. In particular, it has been used in
[
OR07
]
around a triple critical point to obtain the extended Pearcey kernel for weighted random tilings of special domains; in
[
GP19
]
, it was used to prove convergence to the extended discrete Sine kernel in the bulk of NBRW.
Our task here is more intricate than these previous works, due to the following reasons. (1) Compared to
[
OR07
]
, we work with general initial configurations rather than special ones. (2) Compared to
[
GP19
]
where the key saddle points are a pair of complex conjugate critical points away from the real line, here we need to handle three critical points near
z_{c}\in\mathbb{R}
, which can lead to more complicated behaviors for
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}
. (3) The fact that we seek for a ‘small distance’ result (i.e., having a cusp at time
nt_{c}
, which is allowed to be much smaller than
n
) adds to the technical difficulty.
Therefore, delicate computations are needed to achieve the desired approximation of
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}
in the inner part of the contours.
For the outer part of the contours, which are taken to be the steepest descent curves of
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\mathsf{D}_{2}
outside a ball of radius
t_{c}n^{-1/4}
around
z_{c}
, it is hard to precisely describe them, as controlling the behavior of
\mathsf{D}_{1}
,
\mathsf{D}_{2}
is challenging in this region. For example, at distance
\asymp\overline{t}^{3/2}
from
z_{c}
, there already exist singular points (i.e.,
{\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n
and
{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n
).

In the next two subsections, we will introduce and analyze the
key functions

\mathsf{D}_{1}
,
\mathsf{D}_{2}
, and study their steepest descent curves.
Using them, we finish the proof of Proposition
5.3
in Sections
5.5
and
5.6
.

5.3.
Key functions

We now define two functions
\mathsf{D}_{2}
and
\mathsf{D}_{1}
, through the following expressions:

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\log\Big(z-\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}}{n}\Big)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1}\log\Big(z+\frac{i}{n}\Big)-\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi nz))+z\log(\beta/(1-\beta)),

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\log\Big(z-\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}}{n}\Big)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1}}\log\Big(z+\frac{i}{n}\Big)-\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi nz))+z\log(\beta/(1-\beta)).

We note that these expressions define
\mathsf{D}_{2}
and
\mathsf{D}_{1}
as holomorphic functions in the upper-half complex plane
\mathbb{H}
, up to adding a pure imaginary constant.
They are also analytically extended to
\mathbb{R}\setminus E(\mathsf{D}_{2})
and
\mathbb{R}\setminus E(\mathsf{D}_{1})
, respectively, where

\displaystyle E(\mathsf{D}_{2})

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\left[(\{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}\}_{i=-M}^{N}\cap[{\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+1,{\mathsf{x}}_{2}-1])\cup([\![{-\infty,{\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}]\!]\cup[\![{{\mathsf{x}}_{2},\infty}]\!]\setminus\{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}\}_{i=-M}^{N})\right],

\displaystyle E(\mathsf{D}_{1})

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\left[(\{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}\}_{i=-M}^{N}\cap[{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1},{\mathsf{x}}_{1}])\cup([\![{-\infty,{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-1}]\!]\cup[\![{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+1,\infty}]\!]\setminus\{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}\}_{i=-M}^{N})\right]

are the sets of poles of
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}
and
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{1}
, respectively.
We note that
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\mathsf{D}_{2}
(resp.
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\mathsf{D}_{1}
) is constant in each interval of
\mathbb{R}\setminus E(\mathsf{D}_{2})
(resp.
\mathbb{R}\setminus E(\mathsf{D}_{1})
);
in particular,
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\mathsf{D}_{2}
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\mathsf{D}_{1}
are both constant in
({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)
.
We then choose the pure imaginary constants for
\mathsf{D}_{2}
and
\mathsf{D}_{1}
such that for
z\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\log\left|z-\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}}{n}\right|+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1}\log\left|z+\frac{i}{n}\right|-\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi nz))+z\log(\beta/(1-\beta)),

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}_{1}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\log\left|z-\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}}{n}\right|+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1}}\log\left|z+\frac{i}{n}\right|-\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi nz))+z\log(\beta/(1-\beta)).

In particular, under this choice, we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\mathsf{D}_{2}=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\mathsf{D}_{1}=0
in
({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)
.
Finally, we analytically extend
\mathsf{D}_{2}
and
\mathsf{D}_{1}
to the lower-half plane
\mathbb{H}^{-}
from
\mathbb{H}\cup({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)
.

Now, by a change of variables of
z={\mathsf{z}}/n
and
w={\mathsf{w}}/n
, we can rewrite (
5.11
) as

(5.14)

K^{\textnormal{Bernoulli}}({\mathsf{t}}_{1},{\mathsf{x}}_{1};{\mathsf{t}}_{2},{\mathsf{x}}_{2})=\mathbf{1}_{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}\geq{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}>{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}(-1)^{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1}\binom{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}\\
+(-1)^{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1}\frac{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}!}{({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1)!}\frac{n^{{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-1}}{(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}}\int\limits_{({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+\frac{1}{2})/n-\mathbf{i}\infty}^{({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+\frac{1}{2})/n+\mathbf{i}\infty}{\rm d}z\oint\limits_{\textnormal{all $w$ poles}}\frac{{\rm d}w}{w-z}\exp(n\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)-n\mathsf{D}_{1}(w)),

where the
z
contour is the straight vertical line
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z=({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+\frac{1}{2})/n
traversed upwards, and the
w
contour encircles all the
w
poles, except the pole at
w=z
.

For the rest of this subsection, we derive some estimates of
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}
near
z_{c}
, by approximating them with some easier-to-analyze functions in several steps.

5.3.1.
The function
\mathsf{G}

It would be more convenient to work with a ‘continuous version’ of the functions
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}
, defined as

(5.15)

\begin{split}\mathsf{G}(z)=&\int_{-\infty}^{E_{-}}\log(z-x)\rho_{0}(x){\rm d}x+\int_{E_{+}}^{\infty}\log(x-z)\rho_{0}(x){\rm d}x\\
&+(z+t_{c})\log(z+t_{c})-z\log(-z)+z\log(\beta/(1-\beta)).\end{split}

We first define this function
\mathsf{G}
for
z\in(E_{-},E_{+})
, and then analytically extend it to
\mathbb{C}\setminus((-\infty,E_{-}]\cup[E_{+},\infty))
.
A key advantage of
\mathsf{G}
is that, while each of
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}
should have three critical points very close to
z_{c}
,

\mathsf{G}
has a triple critical point precisely at
z_{c}
, due to our choice of
(x_{c},t_{c})
in Lemma
5.2
.

Lemma 5.4
.

We have
\mathsf{G}^{\prime}(z_{c})=\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime}(z_{c})=\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})=0
, and
\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})=-24t_{c}^{-4}A
.

Proof.

The fact that
\mathsf{G}^{\prime}(z_{c})=\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime}(z_{c})=\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})=0
is follows straightforwardly from (
5.7
), (
5.8
), and (
5.9
).
Also, we have

\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z)=m_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z)+\frac{2}{(t_{c}+z)^{3}}-\frac{2}{z^{3}}=-\int\frac{6\rho_{0}(x)}{(z-x)^{4}}{\rm d}x+\frac{2}{(t_{c}+z)^{3}}-\frac{2}{z^{3}},

so
\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})=-24t_{c}^{-4}A
by (
5.12
).
∎

Lemma
5.4
indicates that, when
|z-z_{c}|
is small, we can approximate
\mathsf{G}(z)
by
\mathsf{G}(z_{c})-t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{4}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

There exists a constant
c>0
, such that for any
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z-z_{c}|\leq ct_{c}^{3/2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}^{\prime}(z)+4t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim t_{c}^{-11/2}|z-z_{c}|^{4},

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}(z)-\mathsf{G}(z_{c})+t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim t_{c}^{-11/2}|z-z_{c}|^{5}.

Proof.

It suffices to bound the fifth derivative of
\mathsf{G}
.
For any
z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus((-\infty,E_{-}]\cup[E_{+},\infty))
, we have

\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z)=m_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z)-\frac{6}{(t_{c}+z)^{4}}+\frac{6}{z^{4}}.

Take any
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z-z_{c}|\leq ct_{c}^{3/2}
. For a sufficiently small
c
, we have
(\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(z)-E_{-})\wedge(E_{+}-\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(z))\asymp t_{c}^{3/2}
by (
5.10
). Then, with (
5.6
) and (
5.10
), we get that
m_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z)\lesssim t_{c}^{-11/2}
and
-\frac{6}{(t_{c}+z)^{4}}+\frac{6}{z^{4}}\lesssim t_{c}^{-4}
. These two estimates imply that
\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z)\lesssim t_{c}^{-11/2}
, which give the bound
\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z)-\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})\lesssim t_{c}^{-11/2}|z-z_{c}|
for any
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z-z_{c}|\leq ct_{c}^{3/2}
.
Then, the conclusion follows from the Taylor expansion of
\mathsf{G}(z)
and Lemma
5.4
.
∎

We next use Lemma
5.4
to deduce some results that were mentioned earlier.

Lemma 5.6
.

We have
A>0
and
A\asymp 1
. In addition, we have
E_{+}<0
,
E_{-}>-t_{c}
, and
E_{+},E_{-}+t_{c}\gtrsim\overline{t}
.

Proof.

By (
5.5
) and (
5.10
), we have
m_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})<0
and
m_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})\asymp\overline{t}^{-4}
. If the following estimate holds,

(5.16)

-m_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})>\frac{3}{2}\left[\frac{2}{(t_{c}+z_{c})^{3}}-\frac{2}{z_{c}^{3}}\right],

then
\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})<0
and
\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})\asymp\overline{t}^{-4}
, which gives that
A>0
and
A\asymp 1
.

Otherwise, if (
5.16
) fails, then we must have
\overline{t}\asymp t_{c}\asymp 1
.
Define

U_{-}=\int_{-\infty}^{z_{c}}\frac{\rho_{0}(x)}{(z_{c}-x)^{2}}{\rm d}x,\quad V_{-}=\int_{-\infty}^{z_{c}}\frac{\rho_{0}(x)}{(z_{c}-x)^{3}}{\rm d}x,\quad U_{+}=\int_{z_{c}}^{\infty}\frac{\rho_{0}(x)}{(x-z_{c})^{2}}{\rm d}x,\quad V_{+}=\int_{z_{c}}^{\infty}\frac{\rho_{0}(x)}{(x-z_{c})^{3}}{\rm d}x.

For simplicity of notations, we denote
P=(t_{c}+z_{c})^{-1}
and
Q=-z_{c}^{-1}
, which are positive by (
5.10
).
Then, equations
\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime}(z_{c})=0
and
\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})=0
can be written as

(5.17)

U_{-}+U_{+}=P+Q,\quad 2V_{-}-2V_{+}=P^{2}-Q^{2}.

Also, denote
G_{-}=2V_{-}-U_{-}^{2}
and
G_{+}=2V_{+}-U_{+}^{2}
(which are positive by
5.7
below).
Then,

(5.18)

G_{-}-G_{+}=P^{2}-Q^{2}-U_{-}^{2}+U_{+}^{2}.

By Lemma
5.7
below, using
V_{-}^{2}\geq U_{-}^{4}/4+U_{-}^{2}G_{-}/2
and
V_{+}^{2}\geq U_{+}^{4}/4+U_{+}^{2}G_{+}/2
, we get that

(5.19)

\begin{split}-\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})&\geq(U_{-}^{3}+U_{+}^{3})/2+6(V_{-}^{2}/U_{-}+V_{+}^{2}/U_{+})-2P^{3}-2Q^{3}\\
&\geq 2(U_{-}^{3}+U_{+}^{3})+3(U_{-}G_{-}+U_{+}G_{+})-2P^{3}-2Q^{3}.\end{split}

Without loss of generality, we assume that
P\leq Q
. If
U_{-}\leq P
or
U_{-}\geq Q
, we have
-\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})\geq 3U_{-}G_{-}+3U_{+}G_{+}
.
Otherwise, we have
U_{-},U_{+}\in(P,Q)
.
Then, we can write the last line of (
5.19
) as

\begin{split}&2(U_{-}^{3}+U_{+}^{3}-P^{3}-Q^{3})+3U_{+}(G_{+}-G_{-})+3(U_{-}+U_{+})G_{-}\\
=&2(U_{-}-P)(U_{-}^{2}+U_{-}P+P^{2}-U_{+}^{2}-U_{+}Q-Q^{2}+3U_{+}(U_{-}+U_{+}))+3(U_{-}+U_{+})G_{-}\geq 3(U_{-}+U_{+})G_{-},\end{split}

where we used (
5.18
) and the first identity in (
5.17
) in the first step, and the second step simply uses
P\geq 0
and
Q\leq U_{-}+U_{+}
.
From Assumption
5.1
and (
5.10
), it is straightforward to check that similarly to (
5.2
), we have
U_{-},U_{+}\asymp\overline{t}^{-1}
.
Using Lemma
5.7
below and the fact that
\rho_{0}
is supported in an order
1
interval, we obtain that
G_{-}\gtrsim U_{-}\gtrsim\overline{t}^{-1}
and
G_{+}\gtrsim U_{+}\gtrsim\overline{t}^{-1}
.
Thus, we have
-\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})>0
and
-\mathsf{G}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}(z_{c})\gtrsim\overline{t}^{-2}
.
Since we have assumed that
\overline{t}\asymp t_{c}\asymp 1
, we still get that
A>0
and
A\asymp 1
.

We now deduce that
E_{+}<0
with
E_{+}\gtrsim\overline{t}
. Otherwise,
using that
\rho_{0}(x)\in[0,1]
and the growth of
\rho_{0}
in Assumption
5.1
,
we can deduce that
U_{+}<\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(x-z_{c})^{2}}{\rm d}x=Q
and
V_{+}<\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(x-z_{c})^{3}}{\rm d}x=Q^{2}/2
.
Therefore, by (
5.17
), we have
U_{-}>P
while
2V_{-}<P^{2}
, contradicting Lemma
5.7
below. Similarly, we can deduce that
E_{-}>-t_{c}
with
E_{-}+t_{c}\gtrsim\overline{t}
.
∎

The following elementary lemma is used in the proof of Lemma
5.6
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Take any
r>0
and function
\eta:(0,r)\to[0,1]
, denote
U=\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\eta(x){\rm d}x}{x^{2}}
,
V=\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\eta(x){\rm d}x}{x^{3}}
, and
W=\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\eta(x){\rm d}x}{x^{4}}
.
Then, we have
2V-U^{2}\geq 2Ur^{-1}
and
W\geq U^{3}/12+V^{2}/U
.

Proof.

Note that
\eta\mapsto\int_{0}^{r}\frac{\eta(x)}{x^{i}}{\rm d}x
, for
i\in\{2,3,4\}
, are linear functionals. It would then be straightforward to deduce that, given
U<\infty
,
V
is minimized when
\eta
is the indicator function of an interval
(a,r)
, for some
0<a<r
.
In this case, we have
U=a^{-1}-r^{-1}
and
V=a^{-2}/2-r^{-2}/2
, so
2V-U^{2}=2(ar)^{-1}-2r^{-2}=2Ur^{-1}
.

Similarly, given
U,V<\infty
,
W
is minimized when
\eta
is the characteristic function of an interval
(a,b)
, for some
0<a<b\leq\infty
. Then, we have
U=a^{-1}-b^{-1}
,
V=(a^{-2}-b^{-2})/2
,
W=(a^{-3}-b^{-3})/3
.
These imply that
a^{-1}=U/2+V/U
and
b^{-1}=-U/2+V/U
, so
W=U^{3}/12+V^{2}/U
.
∎

5.3.2.
Discrete approximation

We next use the above-obtained information on
\mathsf{G}
to extract properties of
\mathsf{D}_{2}
and
\mathsf{D}_{1}
.
As a first step, we discretize
\mathsf{G}
.
For the rest of this section, we denote
{\mathsf{t}}_{c}=\lfloor t_{c}n\rfloor
.
Define

(5.20)

\overline{\mathsf{G}}(z)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\log\Big(z-\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}}{n}\Big)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=0}^{{\mathsf{t}}_{c}-1}\log\Big(z+\frac{i}{n}\Big)-\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi nz))+z\log(\beta/(1-\beta)).

As in the case of
\mathsf{D}_{2}
and
\mathsf{D}_{1}
, this expression defines a holomorphic function on
\mathbb{H}
, up to adding a pure imaginary constant, and it can be analytically extended to
\mathbb{R}\setminus E(\overline{\mathsf{G}})
from
\mathbb{H}
, where

E(\overline{\mathsf{G}})=n^{-1}\left[(\{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}\}_{i=-M}^{N}\cap[-{\mathsf{t}}_{c}+1,0])\cup((-\infty,-{\mathsf{t}}_{c}]\cup[1,\infty)\setminus\{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}\}_{i=-M}^{N})\right].

We then choose the pure imaginary constant to ensure
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\overline{\mathsf{G}}=0
in the interval
({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)
. Finally, we analytically extend
\overline{\mathsf{G}}
to
\mathbb{H}^{-}
from
\mathbb{H}\cup({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)
.
For the next two lemmas, we show that
\overline{\mathsf{G}}
is a good approximation of
\mathsf{G}
, by bounding the difference between their derivatives.

Lemma 5.8
.

For any
z\in\mathbb{H}
, we have

\mathsf{G}^{\prime}(z)-\overline{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}(z)\lesssim\frac{n^{-1+\epsilon_{4}}}{\inf_{x\in(-\infty,E_{-}\vee({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n)]\cup[E_{+}\wedge({\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n),\infty)}|x-z|}.

Proof.

By (
5.15
) and (
5.20
), we can write
\mathsf{G}^{\prime}(z)-\overline{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}(z)
for
z\in\mathbb{H}
as

(5.21)

\int\frac{\rho_{0}(x)}{z-x}{\rm d}x+\log(z+t_{c})-\log(-z)-\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\frac{1}{nz-{\mathsf{d}}_{i}}-\sum_{i=0}^{{\mathsf{t}}_{c}-1}\frac{1}{nz+i}+\pi\cot(\pi nz),

which is defined first for
({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)\cap(E_{-},E_{+})
, and then analytically extended to
\mathbb{H}
.
We note that

(5.22)

\pi\cot(\pi nz)=\lim_{m\to\infty}\sum_{i\in\llbracket-m,m\rrbracket}\frac{1}{nz+i},

for any
z\in\mathbb{H}
, so we have

\displaystyle\Big|\log(z+t_{c})-\log(-z)-\sum_{i=0}^{{\mathsf{t}}_{c}-1}\frac{1}{nz+i}+\pi\cot(\pi nz)\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\Big|-\int_{-m}^{0}\frac{1}{nz+x}{\rm d}x-\int_{nt_{c}}^{m}\frac{1}{nz+x}{\rm d}x+\sum_{i=-m}^{-1}\frac{1}{nz+i}+\sum_{i={\mathsf{t}}_{c}}^{m}\frac{1}{nz+i}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=-\infty}^{-1}\Big|\int_{i}^{i+1}\frac{1}{nz+x}{\rm d}x-\frac{1}{nz+i}\Big|+\sum_{i={\mathsf{t}}_{c}}^{\infty}\Big|\int_{i+nt_{c}-{\mathsf{t}}_{c}}^{i+nt_{c}-{\mathsf{t}}_{c}+1}\frac{1}{nz+x}{\rm d}x-\frac{1}{nz+i}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=-\infty}^{-1}\frac{1}{\inf_{x\in[i,i+1]}|nz+x|^{2}}+\sum_{i={\mathsf{t}}_{c}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\inf_{x\in[i,i+2]}|nz+x|^{2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{n\inf_{x\in(-\infty,{\mathsf{t}}_{c}/n]\cup[0,\infty)}|x-z|}.

We next bound the remaining terms in (
5.21
).
Recall the quantiles
\gamma_{i}
,
i\in[\![{-M,N}]\!]
, defined in Assumption
5.1
.
We have

(5.23)

\Big|\int\frac{\rho_{0}(x)}{z-x}{\rm d}x-\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\frac{1}{nz-{\mathsf{d}}_{i}}\Big|\leq\Big|\sum_{i=-M}^{N-1}\int_{\gamma_{i}}^{\gamma_{i+1}}\rho_{0}(x)\Big(\frac{1}{z-x}-\frac{1}{z-{\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n}\Big){\rm d}x\Big|+\Big|\frac{1}{nz-{\mathsf{d}}_{N}}\Big|.

For each
i\in\llbracket-M,N-1\rrbracket
and
x\in[\gamma_{i},\gamma_{i+1}]
, we have

\Big|\frac{1}{z-x}-\frac{1}{z-{\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n}\Big|\leq\int_{x\wedge({\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n)}^{x\vee({\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n)}\frac{{\rm d}y}{|z-y|^{2}}.

Thus, (
5.23
) can be bounded by

(5.24)

\sum_{i=-M}^{N-1}\int_{\gamma_{i}\wedge({\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n)}^{\gamma_{i+1}^{\prime}\vee({\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n)}\frac{{\rm d}y}{n|z-y|^{2}}+\Big|\frac{1}{nz-{\mathsf{d}}_{N}}\Big|,

where we denote
\gamma_{0}^{\prime}=E_{-}
(in contrast to
\gamma_{0}=E_{+}
) and
\gamma_{i}^{\prime}=\gamma_{i}
for
i\in\llbracket-M,N\rrbracket\setminus\{0\}
.

For each
i\in[\![{-M,N}]\!]
and
y\in[\gamma_{i}\wedge({\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n),\gamma_{i+1}^{\prime}\vee({\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n)]
, we have either
\gamma_{i}\leq y\leq{\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n
or
{\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n\leq y\leq\gamma_{i+1}^{\prime}
.
We note that by (
5.1
), for any
y\in(-\infty,E_{-}\vee({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n)]\cup[E_{+}\wedge({\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n),\infty)
we have

|\{i\in\llbracket-M,N-1\rrbracket:\gamma_{i}\leq y\leq{\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n\}|+|\{i\in\llbracket-M,N-1\rrbracket:{\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n\leq y\leq\gamma_{i+1}^{\prime}\}|\lesssim n^{\epsilon_{4}};

and for
y\in(E_{-}\vee({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n),E_{+}\wedge({\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n))
we have

|\{i\in\llbracket-M,N-1\rrbracket:\gamma_{i}\leq y\leq{\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n\}|+|\{i\in\llbracket-M,N-1\rrbracket:{\mathsf{d}}_{i}/n\leq y\leq\gamma_{i+1}^{\prime}\}|=0.

Thus, we can further bound (
5.24
) by

\int_{(-\infty,E_{-}\vee({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n)]\cup[E_{+}\wedge({\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n),\infty)}\frac{n^{-1+\epsilon_{4}}{\rm d}y}{|z-y|^{2}}+\Big|\frac{1}{nz-{\mathsf{d}}_{N}}\Big|,

so the conclusion follows.
∎

Combining Lemmas
5.5
and
5.8
, we can deduce the following result.

Lemma 5.9
.

For any
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z-z_{c}|<ct_{c}^{3/2}
, we have that

\overline{\mathsf{G}}(z)-\overline{\mathsf{G}}(z_{c})+t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{4}\lesssim t_{c}^{-11/2}|z-z_{c}|^{5}+n^{-1+\epsilon_{4}}t_{c}^{-3/2}|z-z_{c}|.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.8
, for any
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z-z_{c}|<ct_{c}^{3/2}
, we have
|\overline{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}(z)-\mathsf{G}^{\prime}(z)|\lesssim n^{-1+\epsilon_{4}}t_{c}^{-3/2}
. Then, by integrating over
z
, we get
|\overline{\mathsf{G}}(z)-\mathsf{G}(z)-\overline{\mathsf{G}}(z_{c})+\mathsf{G}(z_{c})|\lesssim n^{-1+\epsilon_{4}}t_{c}^{-3/2}|z-z_{c}|
. Together with Lemma
5.5
, this concludes the proof.
∎

5.3.3.
Estimates of
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}

We next show that
\overline{\mathsf{G}}
is close to
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}
.

Lemma 5.10
.

For any
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z-z_{c}|\leq ct_{c}
, we have

\overline{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}(z)-\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{1}(z),\ \overline{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}(z)-\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)\lesssim n^{-3/4}t_{c}^{-1}+n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-2}|z-z_{c}|.

Proof.

For
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z-z_{c}|\leq ct_{c}
, we can write that

\overline{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}(z)-\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)=\sum_{i=0}^{{\mathsf{t}}_{c}-1}\frac{1}{nz+i}-\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1}\frac{1}{nz+i}.

Since
{\mathsf{x}}_{2},{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{c}\lesssim n^{1/2}
and
|z-z_{c}|\leq ct_{c}
(so
z,z+t_{c}\asymp t_{c}
according to (
5.10
)), we estimate it as

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1}{nz}\right)-\log\left(\frac{nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}{nz+{\mathsf{t}}_{c}}\right)+\OO\Big(\int_{0\wedge(-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1)}^{0\vee(-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1)}\frac{{\rm d}y}{|nz+y|^{2}}\Big)+\OO\Big(\int_{{\mathsf{t}}_{c}\wedge(-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})}^{{\mathsf{t}}_{c}\vee(-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})}\frac{{\rm d}y}{|nz+y|^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\log\left(\frac{(nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1)(nz+{\mathsf{t}}_{c})}{nz(nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})}\right)+\OO(n^{-3/2}t_{c}^{-2}).

We note that

\log\left(\frac{(nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1)(nz+{\mathsf{t}}_{c})}{nz(nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})}\right)=\log\left(1+\frac{nz({\mathsf{t}}_{c}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+1)+(-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1){\mathsf{t}}_{c}}{nz(nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})}\right).

Again, using that
{\mathsf{x}}_{2},{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{c}\lesssim n^{1/2}
and
z,z+t_{c}\asymp t_{c}
, we get that

\frac{nz_{c}({\mathsf{t}}_{c}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+1)+(-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1){\mathsf{t}}_{c}}{n^{2}t_{c}^{2}}+\OO(n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-2}|z-z_{c}|)=\OO(n^{-3/4}t_{c}^{-1}+n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-2}|z-z_{c}|),

where we used that
{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+\frac{z_{c}}{t_{c}}({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{c})={\mathsf{x}}_{2}-B({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{c})\lesssim n^{1/4}.

Thus, we have

\overline{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}(z)-\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)\lesssim n^{-3/2}t_{c}^{-2}+n^{-3/4}t_{c}^{-1}+n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-2}|z-z_{c}|.

Since
n^{-1/2+\epsilon_{1}}<\overline{t}\asymp t_{c}
, we have
n^{-3/2}t_{c}^{-2}\lesssim n^{-3/4}t_{c}^{-1}
, which gives the desired bound for
\overline{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}(z)-\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)
.
The bound for
\overline{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}(z)-\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{1}(z)
is proved similarly.
∎

Putting together Lemmas
5.5
,
5.8
, and
5.10
, we get the following estimate for
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}
near
z_{c}
.

Lemma 5.11
.

For any
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z-z_{c}|\leq ct_{c}^{3/2}
, we have

\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{1}(z)+4t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{3},\ \mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)+4t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{3}\lesssim n^{-3/4}t_{c}^{-1}+n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-2}|z-z_{c}|+t_{c}^{-11/2}|z-z_{c}|^{4}.

Note that here we use that
n^{-1+\epsilon_{4}}t_{c}^{-3/2}\lesssim n^{-3/4}t_{c}^{-1}
(since
n^{-1/2+\epsilon_{1}}<\overline{t}\asymp t_{c}
, and
\epsilon_{4}
is chosen small enough depending on
\epsilon_{1}
).

5.3.4.
Away from
z_{c}

So far, we have obtained estimates on
\mathsf{D}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}^{\prime}
near
z_{c}
, using the approximations
\overline{\mathsf{G}}
and
\mathsf{G}
.
We will also need some estimates on
\mathsf{D}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}^{\prime}
away from
z_{c}
, which are stated as follows.

Lemma 5.12
.

For any
z\in\mathbb{H}
, suppose that
x_{*}
is the element in
E(\mathsf{D}_{2})
with the smallest
|z-x_{*}|
. Then, we have that

\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)-\frac{s}{n(z-x_{*})}\lesssim\log n,

for some
s\in\{1,-1\}
, depending on the residue of
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}
at
x_{*}
.
A similar estimate holds for
\mathsf{D}_{1}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We only prove the bound for
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)
, and the bound for
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{1}(z)
follows from a similar argument.
With the definition of
\mathsf{D}_{2}
and (
5.22
), we can write that

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\frac{1}{nz-{\mathsf{d}}_{i}}+\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1}\frac{1}{nz+i}-\pi\cot(\pi nz)+\log(\beta/(1-\beta))

(5.25)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\frac{1}{nz-{\mathsf{d}}_{i}}-\lim_{m\to\infty}\Big(\sum_{i=-m}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}\frac{1}{nz+i}+\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}^{m}\frac{1}{nz+i}\Big)+\log(\beta/(1-\beta)).

Thus, we have

\Big|\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)-\frac{s}{n(z-x_{*})}\Big|\leq\sum_{x\in E(\mathsf{D}_{2})\cap(-C_{0},C_{0}),x\neq x_{*}}\frac{1}{n|z-x|}+\lim_{m\to\infty}\Big|\sum_{i\in[\![{-m,m}]\!],|i|\geq C_{0}n,i\neq x_{*}n}\frac{1}{nz-i}\Big|+|\log(\beta/(1-\beta))|,

where
C_{0}>0
is a large enough constant so that
-C_{0}n<2{\mathsf{d}}_{-M}<2{\mathsf{d}}_{N}<C_{0}n
.
The first term is bounded by

\sum_{x\in E(\mathsf{D}_{2})\cap(-C_{0},C_{0}),x\neq x_{*}}\frac{1}{n|z-x|}\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{\lceil 2C_{0}n\rceil}\frac{1}{i-1/2}\lesssim\log n.

We also have that

\sum_{i\in[\![{-m,m}]\!],|i|\geq C_{0}n,i\neq x_{*}n}\frac{1}{nz-i}=\int_{I}\frac{{\rm d}y}{nz+y}+\OO\Big(\int_{I}\frac{{\rm d}y}{|nz+y|^{2}}\Big),

where
I
is the union of
[i-1,i]
for all
i\in[\![{-m,m}]\!],|i|\geq C_{0}n,i<x_{*}n
, and
[i,i+1]
for all
i\in[\![{-m,m}]\!],|i|\geq C_{0}n,i>x_{*}n
.
It is straightforward to check that

\lim_{m\to\infty}\int_{I}\frac{{\rm d}y}{nz+y}=\OO(\log n),\quad\int_{I}\frac{{\rm d}y}{|nz+y|^{2}}=\OO(1),

and the conclusion follows.
∎

When
|z|
is large, we can directly approximate
\mathsf{D}_{1}(z)
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)
using the linear function
z\mapsto z[\pi\mathbf{i}+\log(\beta/(1-\beta))]
,
as follows.

Lemma 5.13
.

For any
z\in\mathbb{H}
with
|z|>n
, we have

\mathsf{D}_{1}(z)-z[\pi\mathbf{i}+\log(\beta/(1-\beta))],\ \mathsf{D}_{2}(z)-z[\pi\mathbf{i}+\log(\beta/(1-\beta))]\lesssim[-n^{-1}\log(\min_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}|nz-i|)]\vee 0+\log|z|.

Proof.

We only prove the bound for
\mathsf{D}_{2}
, while the proof for
\mathsf{D}_{1}
is similar.
Since
M,N\asymp n
,
|{\mathsf{d}}_{-M}|,|{\mathsf{d}}_{N}|\asymp n
,
|{\mathsf{x}}_{2}|,|{\mathsf{t}}_{2}|\lesssim n
, and
|z|>n
, we have

(5.26)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\log\Big(z-\frac{{\mathsf{d}}_{i}}{n}\Big)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1}\log\Big(z+\frac{i}{n}\Big)\lesssim\log|z|,

where the left-hand side is defined to be holomorphic in
\mathbb{H}
and real near
(2n)^{-1}
.
Next, we consider
-\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi nz))
, which is defined first for
z\in(0,n^{-1})
, and then analytically extended to
\mathbb{C}\setminus((-\infty,0]\cup[n^{-1},\infty))
.
We note that
\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi nz))+z\pi\mathbf{i}
is periodic:
for any
z\in\mathbb{H}
, we have
\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi nz))+z\pi\mathbf{i}=\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi n(z+2n^{-1})))+(z+2n^{-1})\pi\mathbf{i}
.
This gives
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}[\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi nz))+z\pi\mathbf{i}]\lesssim n^{-1}
for any
z\in\mathbb{H}
.
For
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}[\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi nz))+z\pi\mathbf{i}]
, it is equal to

\frac{1}{n}\log(|\sin(\pi nz)e^{\mathbf{i}\pi nz}|)=\frac{1}{n}\log\Big(\Big|\frac{e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}nz}-1}{2\mathbf{i}}\Big|\Big),

which is of order
\OO(n^{-1})
when
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z>n^{-1}/5
, and of order
\OO(n^{-1}(1-\log(\min_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}|nz-i|)))
when
0<\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(z)\leq n^{-1}/5
.
Thus, we conclude that for any
z\in\mathbb{H}
,

\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi nz))+z\pi\mathbf{i}\lesssim[-n^{-1}\log(\min_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}|nz-i|)]\vee 0+n^{-1}.

Finally, to get
\mathsf{D}_{2}
from the left-hand side of (
5.26
) (which is taken to be real near
(2n)^{-1}
) and
-\frac{1}{n}\log(\sin(\pi nz))
(which is taken to be real in
(0,n^{-1})
), one only needs to add a pure imaginary number, which is
\OO(1)
since
M,N\asymp n
.
Therefore, the conclusion follows.
∎

5.4.
Contour deformation

To obtain the estimates of kernels and prove Proposition
5.3
, we will use the steepest descent method. For this purpose, we need to deform the contours for
K^{\textnormal{Bernoulli}}
in (
5.14
).
In this subsection, we construct these deformed contours.

From the above computations on
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}
, we expect to deform the contours so that both pass through
z_{c}
, and the integrand in (
5.14
) decays fast for
z
and
w
away from
z_{c}
.
Specifically, we consider the contours inside and outside
\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z-z_{c}|\leq n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\}
separately. (Recall that
\epsilon_{2}
is one of the parameters defined at the beginning of this section.)
We now record a useful lemma.

Lemma 5.14
.

For any
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z-z_{c}|\lesssim n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
, we have

\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{1}(z)+4t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{3},\;\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)+4t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{3}\lesssim n^{-3/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}^{-1},

\mathsf{D}_{1}(z)-\mathsf{D}_{1}(z_{c})+t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{4},\;\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)-\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c})+t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{4}\lesssim n^{-1+2\epsilon_{2}}.

The first estimate is directly implied by Lemma
5.11
and the facts that
n^{-1/2+\epsilon_{1}}<\overline{t}\asymp t_{c}
.
The second estimate is obtained by integrating over
z
.

For the rest of this section, we use
[z_{1}\to\cdots\to z_{k}]
to denote the contour obtained by connecting
z_{1},\ldots,z_{k}\in\mathbb{C}
sequentially using straight line segments.
In such notations, we may also take
z_{1}
or
z_{k}
to be
\infty e^{\pi\mathbf{i}\theta}
for some
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
, in which case the first or last segment is an infinite straight line in the corresponding direction.
Our first step is the following deformation.

Lemma 5.15
.

For (
5.14
), the contours can be replaced by the followings: the
w
contour is the union of

(5.27)

\displaystyle[z_{c}\to z_{c}+e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}

\displaystyle\to-t_{c}-1\to z_{c}+e^{5\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to z_{c}],

(5.28)

\displaystyle[z_{c}\to z_{c}+e^{7\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}

\displaystyle\to 1\to z_{c}+e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to z_{c}],

and the
z
contour is the straight vertical line passing through
z_{c}
, traversed upwards.

Proof.

We first assume that the contour of
w
in (
5.14
) is taken to be small circles around the
w
poles.
Then we fix
w
and deform the contour of
z
, from the vertical line through
({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+1/2)/n
to the vertical line through
z_{c}
.
It is straightforward to check that, by Lemma
5.13
, the integral over
z
along
[({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+1/2)/n+\mathbf{i}X\to z_{c}+\mathbf{i}X]
for some
X\in\mathbb{R}
would
\to 0
as
|X|\to\infty
.
Thus, it remains to consider the poles of
z
.

We note that there is no pole of
z
in
[({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+1/2)/n,z_{c}]
, except for
w
(when
w
is in a small circle around a point in this interval).
For the residue at
z=w
, it can be written as a coefficient multiplying

\oint\limits_{\textnormal{all $w$ poles in }[({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+1/2)/n,z_{c}]}\frac{(nw-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1)_{{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1}}{(nw-{\mathsf{x}}_{1})_{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}+1}}{\rm d}w,

which vanishes since the integrand has no
w
pole in
[({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+1/2)/n,z_{c}]
.
Thus we are done with deforming the contour of
z
.

For
w
, the contours (
5.27
) and (
5.28
) enclose all the
w
poles
(\{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1},{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1}+1,\ldots,{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-1,{\mathsf{x}}_{1}\}\cap\{{\mathsf{d}}_{-M},\ldots,{\mathsf{d}}_{N}\})/n
, since
-nt_{c}-n<{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1}<{\mathsf{x}}_{1}<n
.
Hence,
w
can be integrated along the union of (
5.27
) and (
5.28
).
∎

5.4.1.
Steepest descent curves

For the part of the contours (
5.27
), (
5.28
) and
\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(z)=z_{c}\}
outside
\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z-z_{c}|\leq n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\}
,
we will further deform them to follow the steepest descent curves of
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(\mathsf{D}_{1})
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(\mathsf{D}_{2})
.
For this purpose, we need to analyze the critical points of
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}
.
Define

\Gamma_{l}=(-\infty,z_{c}-n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}]\cup[z_{c}+n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c},\infty),\;\Gamma_{c}=\{z:\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(z)\geq 0,|z-z_{c}|=n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\},

and let
\Gamma=\Gamma_{l}\cup\Gamma_{c}
.
Let
U=\{z:\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(z)\geq 0,|z-z_{c}|\geq n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\}
.
Note
\Gamma
is the boundary of
U
.

Lemma 5.16
.

The functions
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}
have no critical point in the interior of
U
.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)
can be written as (
5.25
) for
z\in\mathbb{H}
.
By Hurwitz’s theorem, it suffices to show that for all large enough
m
,

(5.29)

\sum_{i=-M}^{N}\frac{1}{nz-{\mathsf{d}}_{i}}-\sum_{i=-m}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}\frac{1}{nz+i}-\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}^{m}\frac{1}{nz+i}+\log\frac{\beta}{1-\beta}

has no zero in the interior of
U
.
For this purpose, we multiply (
5.29
) by
\prod_{x\in E(\mathsf{D}_{2})\cap[-m/n,m/n]}(z-x)
, and obtain a polynomial with degree at most
|E(\mathsf{D}_{2})\cap[-m/n,m/n]|
.
So (
5.29
) has at most
|E(\mathsf{D}_{2})\cap[-m/n,m/n]|
many zeros.

Consider the poles of (
5.29
), i.e.,
E(\mathsf{D}_{2})\cap[-m/n,m/n]
, which divide
\mathbb{R}
into
|E(\mathsf{D}_{2})\cap[-m/n,m/n]|+1
many intervals. By (
5.13
), except for at most four of these intervals (the left-most and right-most open intervals, and two intervals in the middle where the residues of the poles change signs), there is at least one zero in each interval.
By Rouché’s theorem and Lemma
5.14
, we see that there are precisely three zeros inside
\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z-z_{c}|<n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\}
.
Now, we have found at least
|E(\mathsf{D}_{2})\cap[-m/n,m/n]|
many zeros of (
5.29
) in
\mathbb{R}\cup\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z-z_{c}|<n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\}
. Hence,
\mathsf{D}_{2}^{\prime}
has no zero in the interior of
U
.

The statement for
\mathsf{D}_{1}
follows a similar argument.
∎

Since
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(\mathsf{D}_{2})
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2})
are harmonic conjugates, the steepest descent curves of
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(\mathsf{D}_{2})
starting from around
z_{c}
are given by the set
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\mathsf{D}_{2}=0
, which can be described as follows.

Lemma 5.17
.

The set
\mathfrak{A}=\{z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus E(\mathsf{D}_{2}):\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z))=0\}\cap U
contains the following parts (cf. Figure
6
):

(1)

\Gamma_{l}\cap({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)
;

(2)

Two open intervals
P_{2,-}\subset(-\infty,({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2})/n)
and
P_{2,+}\subset({\mathsf{x}}_{2}/n,\infty)
, defined as

P_{2,-}:=\left\{x\in\mathfrak{A},x<({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2})/n:|\{-M\leq i\leq-1:{\mathsf{d}}_{i}>xn\}|=|\{i\in\mathbb{Z}:xn<i\leq{\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}\}|\right\},

P_{2,+}:=\left\{x\in\mathfrak{A},x>{\mathsf{x}}_{2}/n:|\{0\leq i\leq N:{\mathsf{d}}_{i}<xn\}|=|\{i\in\mathbb{Z}:{\mathsf{x}}_{2}\leq i<xn\}|\right\}.

By (
5.13
),
P_{2,-}
and
P_{2,+}
are non-empty finite intervals.

(3)

Three disjoint, smooth, and self-avoiding curves
\ell_{2,1}
,
\ell_{2,2}
, and
\ell_{2,3}
, such that
\ell_{2,1}
is from
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{1}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\in\Gamma_{c}
to some
z_{-}\in P_{2,-}
,
\ell_{2,3}
is from
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{3}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\in\Gamma_{c}
to some
z_{+}\in P_{2,+}
, and
\ell_{2,2}
is from
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\in\Gamma_{c}
to
\infty
.
Here,
\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\theta_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
and satisfy

(5.30)

|\theta_{3}-\pi/4|,|\theta_{2}-\pi/2|,|\theta_{1}-3\pi/4|\lesssim n^{-2\epsilon_{2}}.

Except for the endpoints, these curves (
\ell_{2,1}
,
\ell_{2,2}
, and
\ell_{2,3}
) are contained in the interior of
U
.

Starting from
\Gamma_{c}
,
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\mathsf{D}_{2}
is strictly decreasing along
\ell_{2,2}
, and strictly increasing along each of
\ell_{2,1}
and
\ell_{2,3}
. Moreover, we have

(5.31)

\begin{split}&\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{1}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})-\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c})-An^{-1+4\epsilon_{2}}\lesssim n^{-1+2\epsilon_{2}},\\
&\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})-\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c})+An^{-1+4\epsilon_{2}}\lesssim n^{-1+2\epsilon_{2}},\\
&\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{3}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})-\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c})-An^{-1+4\epsilon_{2}}\lesssim n^{-1+2\epsilon_{2}}.\end{split}

Similar statements hold for
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and the set
\{z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus E(\mathsf{D}_{1}):\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{1}(z))=0\}\cap U
.

z_{c}
{\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n
{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n
({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2})/n
{\mathsf{x}}_{2}/n
z_{-}
z_{+}
P_{2,-}
P_{2,+}
\ell_{2.1}
\ell_{2.2}
\ell_{2.3}

Figure 6
.
An illustration of the set
\{z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus E(\mathsf{D}_{2}):\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z))=0\}\cap U
.

Proof.

It is straightforward to check (from the definition of
\mathsf{D}_{2}
) that for
z\in\Gamma_{l}\cap({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)
or
z\in P_{2,-}\cup P_{2,+}
, we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z))=0
, and
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z))\neq 0
for all other
z\in\Gamma_{l}\setminus E(\mathsf{D}_{2})
.

We next consider the half-circle
\Gamma_{c}
.
Define the function
f:[0,\pi]\to\mathbb{R}
as

f:\theta\mapsto\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})).

Note that
f(0)=f(\pi)=0
.
By Lemma
5.14
, we have

f^{\prime}(\theta)+4\cos(4\theta)n^{-1+4\epsilon_{2}}A\lesssim n^{-1+2\epsilon_{2}}.

With this estimate, we can conclude that
f
has five zeros
0<\theta_{3}<\theta_{2}<\theta_{1}<\pi
, and they satisfy (
5.30
) and (
5.31
).
Using Lemma
5.14
again, we get that

(5.32)

\begin{split}\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{3}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})&=-4Ae^{3\mathbf{i}\pi/4}n^{-3/4+3\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}^{-1}(1+\OO(n^{-2\epsilon_{2}})),\\
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})&=-4Ae^{3\mathbf{i}\pi/2}n^{-3/4+3\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}^{-1}(1+\OO(n^{-2\epsilon_{2}})),\\
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{1}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})&=-4Ae^{\mathbf{i}\pi/4}n^{-3/4+3\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}^{-1}(1+\OO(n^{-2\epsilon_{2}})).\end{split}

It is straightforward to check that
\mathsf{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}>0
on
P_{2,-}
and
P_{2,+}
, so
\mathsf{D}_{2}
has a unique critical point inside each of
P_{2,-}
and
P_{2,+}
, and we denote them by
z_{-}
and
z_{+}
, respectively.

As
\mathsf{D}_{2}
is holomorphic and contains no critical point in the interior of
U
by Lemma
5.16
,
for each
z_{*}
in the interior of
U
, if
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{*}))=0
, one can then take the steepest descent curve of
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\mathsf{D}_{2}
from
z_{*}
.
Along this curve
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\mathsf{D}_{2}=0
, and
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\mathsf{D}_{2}
is strictly monotone due to the absence of critical points.
This curve inside
U
is smooth and self-avoiding; in each direction, it either ends at one of
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{1}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
,
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
,
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{3}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
, or ends at a critical point of
\mathsf{D}_{2}
in
P_{2,-}
or
P_{2,+}
(i.e., one of
z_{-}
and
z_{+}
), or goes to
\infty
. All such curves do not intersect each other.

Consider the
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\mathsf{D}_{2}
steepest descent curves starting from
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{1}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
,
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
,
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{3}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
, and
z_{-}
,
z_{+}
, towards the interior of
U
.
Due to the above estimates (
5.32
) on
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}
, and the fact
\mathsf{D}^{\prime\prime}_{2}>0
on
P_{2,-}
and
P_{2,+}
, we observe that
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\mathsf{D}_{2}
is decreasing along the curves from
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
and
z_{-}
,
z_{+}
, while
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\mathsf{D}_{2}
is increasing along the curves from
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{1}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
and
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{3}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
.

By Lemma
5.13
, we have that any
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\mathsf{D}_{2}=0
curve in the ascending direction of
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\mathsf{D}_{2}
cannot go to
\infty
in
U
, and thus must terminate at one of
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c},z_{-},z_{+}
.
These imply that the
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}\mathsf{D}_{2}
steepest descent curves from
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c},z_{-},z_{+}
are all such
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\mathsf{D}_{2}=0
curves,
and we denote them by
\ell_{2,2}
,
\ell_{2,1}
, and
\ell_{2,3}
.

For the
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(\mathsf{D}_{2})
steepest descent curves from
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{1}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
and
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{3}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
, they cannot end at
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
.
This is because, by (
5.31
) we have that

\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{1}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})-\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})-2An^{-1+4\epsilon_{2}}\lesssim n^{-1+2\epsilon_{2}},

so
\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{1}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})>\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})
; similarly, we have
\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{3}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})>\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})
.
Thus, we conclude that
\ell_{2,2}
must go to
\infty
in
U
.
Since
\ell_{2,2}
does not intersect
\ell_{2,1}
or
\ell_{2,3}
in the interior of
U
, we must have that
\ell_{2,1}
connects
z_{-}
and
e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{1}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
, and
\ell_{2,3}
connects
z_{+}
and
e^{\mathbf{i}\theta_{3}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
.
∎

For the convenience of later applications, for the similar statement on
\mathsf{D}_{1}
, we denote the corresponding intervals as
P_{1,-}
and
P_{1,+}
, and the corresponding curves as
\ell_{1,1}
from
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\vartheta_{1}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\in\Gamma_{c}
to some
w_{-}\in P_{1,-}
,
\ell_{1,3}
from
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\vartheta_{3}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\in\Gamma_{c}
to some
w_{+}\in P_{1,+}
, and
\ell_{1,2}
from
z_{c}+e^{\mathbf{i}\vartheta_{2}}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\in\Gamma_{c}
to
\infty
.
Here,
\vartheta_{1},\vartheta_{2},\vartheta_{3}
are real numbers satisfying that

|\vartheta_{3}-\pi/4|,|\vartheta_{2}-\pi/2|,|\vartheta_{1}-3\pi/4|\lesssim n^{-2\epsilon_{2}}.

We next provide a technical lemma that will be used later.

Lemma 5.18
.

The curve
\ell_{1,1}
(resp.
\ell_{1,2}
,
\ell_{1,3}
,
\ell_{2,1}
,
\ell_{2,2}
,
\ell_{2,3}
) is disjoint from the
n^{-3}
neighborhood of
\mathbb{R}\setminus P_{1,-}
(resp.
\mathbb{R}
,
\mathbb{R}\setminus P_{1,+}
,
\mathbb{R}\setminus P_{2,-}
,
\mathbb{R}
,
\mathbb{R}\setminus P_{2,+}
).

Proof.

We first show that
\ell_{2,1}
is disjoint from the
n^{-2}
neighborhood of
E(\mathsf{D}_{2})
.
For any
x_{*}\in E(\mathsf{D}_{2})
, take
z\in\mathbb{H}
such that
|z-x_{*}|<\frac{1}{2n}
. By Lemma
5.12
, we have that

\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)=\frac{s}{n(z-x_{*})}+\OO(\log n),

where
s\in\{1,-1\}
, depending on the residue of
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}
at
x_{*}
.
Without loss of generality, assume that
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z\geq x_{*}
.
Then, by integrating over
z
, we get that

\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z))-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(x_{*}+(2n)^{-1}))=n^{-1}s\arg(z-x_{*})+\OO(\log n\cdot\mathrm{Im}\,z),

where
\arg
takes value in
(0,\pi/2]
as
z-x_{*}\in\mathbb{H}
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(z-x_{*})\geq 0
.
Note that
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}z<\arg(z-x_{*})|z-x_{*}|
. Hence, when
|z-x_{*}|\lesssim n^{-2}
, we have that

\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z))-\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(x_{*}+(2n)^{-1}))=n^{-1}s\arg(z-x_{*})(1+\OO(\log(n)/n))\in(-\pi n^{-1},\pi n^{-1})\setminus\{0\}.

Since
x_{*}+(2n)^{-1}\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(x_{*}+(2n)^{-1}))\in\pi n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
. Thus, we conclude that
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z))\not\in\pi n^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
whenever
|z-x_{*}|\lesssim n^{-2}
.
This implies that
\ell_{2,1}
is at least
n^{-2}
away from
E(\mathsf{D}_{2})
.

For any
z\in\mathbb{H}
and
y\in\mathbb{R}
, by Lemma
5.12
, integrating
\mathsf{D}_{2}^{\prime}(z)
along a curve connecting
y
and
z
yields that

(5.33)

\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)-\mathsf{D}_{2}(y)\lesssim\frac{|z-y|}{\inf_{x\in E(\mathsf{D}_{2})}n(|z-x|\wedge|y-x|)}+\log(n)|z-y|.

If
z
is in the
n^{-3}
neighborhood of
\mathbb{R}\setminus(P_{2,-}\cup P_{2,+}\cup({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n))
but not the
n^{-2}
neighborhood of
E(\mathsf{D}_{2})
, then we must have that
\inf_{x\in E(\mathsf{D}_{2})}|\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z-x|\gtrsim n^{-2}
and
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z\in\mathbb{R}\setminus(P_{2,-}\cup P_{2,+}\cup({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n))
, which give that
|\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z))|\geq\pi n^{-1}
. Then, (
5.33
) for
y=\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z
implies that
|\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)-\mathsf{D}_{2}(\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}z)|\lesssim n^{-2}
, so
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z))\neq 0
, meaning that
z\not\in\ell_{2,1}
.

So far, we have shown that
\ell_{2,1}
is disjoint from the
n^{-3}
neighborhood of
\mathbb{R}\setminus(P_{2,-}\cup P_{2,+}\cup({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n))
, and the
n^{-2}
neighborhood of
E(\mathsf{D}_{2})
.
Similarly, these statements also hold for
\ell_{2,2}
and
\ell_{2,3}
.

We next consider
z\in\mathbb{H}
in the
n^{-3}
neighborhood of
({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)
, while
|z-z_{c}|>n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
and
|z-{\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n|,|z-{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n|\geq n^{-2}
.
Take
y
to be some point in
({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,z_{c}-n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})\cup(z_{c}+n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c},{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)
with
z-y\lesssim n^{-3}
.
By Lemma
5.14
, we have that
\mathsf{D}_{2}^{\prime}>0
in
({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,z_{c}-n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c})
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}^{\prime}<0
in
(z_{c}+n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c},{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)
, and that
\mathsf{D}_{2}(y)-\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c})<-cn^{-1+4\epsilon_{2}}
for a constant
c>0
.
So by (
5.33
), we have

\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z))<\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(y))+\OO(n^{-2})<\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c}))-cn^{4\epsilon_{2}-1}+\OO(n^{-2}).

On the other hand, by Lemma
5.17

(more precisely, (
5.31
) and the fact that
\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(\mathsf{D}_{2})
is increasing along
\ell_{2,1}
and
\ell_{2,3}
from
\Gamma_{c}
), we conclude that
z\not\in\ell_{2,1}\cup\ell_{2,3}
.
In other words,
\ell_{2,1}
and
\ell_{2,3}
are disjoint from the
n^{-3}
neighborhood of
({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)
.
By similar arguments, we can show that
\ell_{2,2}
is disjoint from the
n^{-3}
neighborhood of
P_{2,-}\cup P_{2,+}
.

Since
\ell_{2,1}
,
\ell_{2,2}
,
\ell_{2,3}
are disjoint, by planarity we must have that
\ell_{2,2}
is disjoint from the
n^{-3}
neighborhood of
({\mathsf{d}}_{-1}/n,{\mathsf{d}}_{0}/n)
,

\ell_{2,1}
is disjoint from the
n^{-3}
neighborhood of
P_{2,+}
, and

\ell_{2,3}
is disjoint from the
n^{-3}
neighborhood of
P_{2,-}
.

The results for
\ell_{1,1}
,
\ell_{1,2}
,
\ell_{1,3}
can be proved analogously. Putting all these together concludes the proof.
∎

With all the above preparations (on the properties of
\mathsf{D}_{1}
,
\mathsf{D}_{2}
around
z_{c}
and their steepest descent curves) in the above two subsections, we are now ready to prove the approximation of the NBRW kernel by the Pearcey kernel, i.e., Proposition
5.3
.

5.5.
Convergence to Pearcey

Using (
5.14
) and Lemma
5.15
, Proposition
5.3
can be deduced from the following lemma, plus an estimate of the binomial term
\binom{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}
in Lemma
5.21
below.

Lemma 5.19
.

Under the setting of Proposition
5.3
,
consider the integral

(5.34)

B^{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}(1-B)^{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+{\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}\frac{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}!}{({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1)!}\frac{n^{{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-1}}{(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}}\iint\frac{dwdz}{w-z}\exp(n\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)-n\mathsf{D}_{1}(w)).

We divide the contours into two parts: inside or outside
\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z-z_{c}|\leq n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\}
.

Inner part:

When the
w
contour is taken to be
[z_{c}+e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to z_{c}\to z_{c}+e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}]
and
[z_{c}+e^{5\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to z_{c}\to z_{c}+e^{7\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}]
, and the
z
contour is taken to be
[z_{c}+e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/2}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to z_{c}+e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/2}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}]
, the integral (
5.34
) is equal to

(5.35)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}n^{1/4}A^{1/4}B(1-B)}\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}}\iint\frac{d\mathbf{w}d\mathbf{z}}{\mathbf{w}-\mathbf{z}}\exp\Big(\frac{-\mathbf{z}^{4}+\mathbf{w}^{4}}{4}+\frac{\gamma_{1}\mathbf{w}-\gamma_{2}\mathbf{z}}{\sqrt{2}A^{1/4}B(1-B)}+\frac{\tau_{1}\mathbf{w}^{2}-\tau_{2}\mathbf{z}^{2}}{4A^{1/2}B(1-B)}\Big)\\
+\OO(n^{-1/4-\epsilon_{3}}),

where the
\mathbf{w}
and
\mathbf{z}
contours are respectively

(5.36)

[\infty e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/4}\to 0\to\infty e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/4}]\cup[\infty e^{5\pi\mathbf{i}/4}\to 0\to\infty e^{7\pi\mathbf{i}/4}],\quad[\infty e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/2}\to 0\to\infty e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/2}].

Outer part:

When either (i) the
w
contour is taken to be
[z_{c}+e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to-t_{c}-1\to z_{c}+e^{5\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}]
and
[z_{c}+e^{7\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to 1\to z_{c}+e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}]
, and the
z
contour is taken to be
[\infty e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/2}\to z_{c}\to\infty e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/2}]
, or (ii) the
w
contour is taken to be
[z_{c}+e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to z_{c}\to z_{c}+e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}]
and
[z_{c}+e^{5\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to z_{c}\to z_{c}+e^{7\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}]
, and the
z
contour is taken to be
[\infty e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/2}\to z_{c}+e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/2}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}]
and
[z_{c}+e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/2}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to\infty e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/2}]
, the integral (
5.34
) is
\lesssim\exp(-cn^{4\epsilon_{2}})
.

We next prove the inner part of Lemma
5.19
, and the outer part will be proved in
Section

5.6
.

5.5.1.
Inner contour integral

We first analyze the factor in front of the integral in (
5.34
) at
z=z_{c}
.

Lemma 5.20
.

We have

(5.37)

\log\left(B^{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}(1-B)^{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+{\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}\frac{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}!}{({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1)!}\right)+n\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c})-n\mathsf{D}_{1}(z_{c})\\
=({\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+1)\log n-\log(t_{c}B(1-B))+\OO(n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-1}).

Proof.

The left-hand side of (
5.37
) is equal to

(5.38)

({\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2})\log B+({\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+{\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1})\log(1-B)+\log({\mathsf{t}}_{1}!/({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1)!)\\
+\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1}\log\Big|z_{c}+\frac{i}{n}\Big|-\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1}}\log\Big|z_{c}+\frac{i}{n}\Big|.

Using
{\mathsf{x}}_{1},{\mathsf{x}}_{2},{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-nt_{c},{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c}\lesssim n^{1/2}
and
z_{c},t_{c}+z_{c}\asymp t_{c}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1}\log\Big|z_{c}+\frac{i}{n}\Big|-\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1}}\log\Big|z_{c}+\frac{i}{n}\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\int_{-\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}{n}}^{\frac{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}{n}}\log|z_{c}+x|{\rm d}x-n\int_{-\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}{n}}^{\frac{-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1}}{n}}\log|z_{c}+x|{\rm d}x-\log(-z_{c})-\log(t_{c}+z_{c})+\OO(n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-1}).

The first two terms in the last line are equal to

\displaystyle(-nz_{c}+{\mathsf{x}}_{2})\log\Big(-z_{c}+\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}{n}\Big)-(-nz_{c}+{\mathsf{x}}_{2})+(nz_{c}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})\log\Big(z_{c}+\frac{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}{n}\Big)-(nz_{c}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(-nz_{c}+{\mathsf{x}}_{1})\log\Big(-z_{c}+\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}{n}\Big)+(-nz_{c}+{\mathsf{x}}_{1})-(nz_{c}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1})\log\Big(z_{c}+\frac{-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1}}{n}\Big)+(nz_{c}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1}),

which further simplifies to (recall that
B=-z_{c}t_{c}^{-1}
)

\displaystyle(-nz_{c}+{\mathsf{x}}_{2})\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}{-nz_{c}}\Big)-(-nz_{c}+{\mathsf{x}}_{1})\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}{-nz_{c}}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(nz_{c}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})\log\Big(1+\frac{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c}}{n(z_{c}+t_{c})}\Big)-(nz_{c}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1})\log\Big(1+\frac{-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-nt_{c}}{n(z_{c}+t_{c})}\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle({\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1})\log(B)+(-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1})\log(1-B)+({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{1})\log(t_{c})+{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}.

Using Stirling’s approximation and that
{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-nt_{c},{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c}\lesssim n^{1/2}
, we also get that

\log({\mathsf{t}}_{1}!/({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1)!)={\mathsf{t}}_{1}\log({\mathsf{t}}_{1})-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}\log({\mathsf{t}}_{2})-{\mathsf{t}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+\log(nt_{c})+\OO(n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-1})\\
=({\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+1)\log(nt_{c})+{\mathsf{t}}_{1}\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-nt_{c}}{nt_{c}}\Big)-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c}}{nt_{c}}\Big)-{\mathsf{t}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+\OO(n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-1}).

By summing up the above expressions, we have that (
5.38
) is equal to

(5.39)

\begin{split}&(-nz_{c}+{\mathsf{x}}_{2})\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}{-nz_{c}}\Big)-(-nz_{c}+{\mathsf{x}}_{1})\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}{-nz_{c}}\Big)\\
+&(nz_{c}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})\log\Big(1+\frac{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c}}{n(z_{c}+t_{c})}\Big)-(nz_{c}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1})\log\Big(1+\frac{-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-nt_{c}}{n(z_{c}+t_{c})}\Big)\\
+&{\mathsf{t}}_{1}\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-nt_{c}}{nt_{c}}\Big)-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c}}{nt_{c}}\Big)\\
+&({\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+1)\log n-\log(t_{c}B(1-B))+\OO(n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-1}).\end{split}

The last line matches the right-hand side of (
5.37
).
It remains to show that the first three lines are of order
\OO(n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-1})
.
We first consider

(5.40)

(-nz_{c}+{\mathsf{x}}_{2})\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}{-nz_{c}}\Big)+(nz_{c}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})\log\Big(1+\frac{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c}}{n(z_{c}+t_{c})}\Big)-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c}}{nt_{c}}\Big).

For this expression, its derivative with respect to
{\mathsf{x}}_{2}
is

\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}{-nz_{c}}\Big)-\log\Big(1+\frac{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c}}{n(z_{c}+t_{c})}\Big)=\log\Big(1+\frac{t_{c}({\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}_{2}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2})}{z_{c}(nz_{c}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})}\Big)\lesssim n^{-3/4}t_{c}^{-1},

where
{\mathsf{x}}_{2}^{\prime}=\frac{-z_{c}}{t_{c}}({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c})
and we used that
{\mathsf{x}}_{2}^{\prime}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}\lesssim n^{1/4}
. In addition, we have that that value of (
5.40
) vanishes when we replace
{\mathsf{x}}_{2}
by
{\mathsf{x}}_{2}^{\prime}
, i.e.,

(-nz_{c}+{\mathsf{x}}_{2}^{\prime})\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{2}^{\prime}}{-nz_{c}}\Big)+(nz_{c}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}^{\prime}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})\log\Big(1+\frac{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}^{\prime}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c}}{n(z_{c}+t_{c})}\Big)-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c}}{nt_{c}}\Big)=0.

By integrating over
{\mathsf{x}}_{2}
, we obtain that (
5.40
) is of order
\OO(n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-1})
.
Similarly, we have

(-nz_{c}+{\mathsf{x}}_{1})\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}{-nz_{c}}\Big)+(nz_{c}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1})\log\Big(1+\frac{-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-nt_{c}}{n(z_{c}+t_{c})}\Big)-{\mathsf{t}}_{1}\log\Big(1+\frac{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-nt_{c}}{nt_{c}}\Big)\lesssim n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-1}.

Plugging these two estimates into (
5.39
), the conclusion follows.
∎

We now finish the estimate on the contour integral inside
\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z-z_{c}|\leq n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\}
.

Proof of Lemma
5.19
: Inner part.

By Lemma
5.20
, using
n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-1}\lesssim n^{-\epsilon_{3}}
(due to that
n^{-1/2+\epsilon_{1}}<\overline{t}\asymp t_{c}
), it suffices to prove the same estimate for

(5.41)

\frac{1}{t_{c}B(1-B)}\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}}\iint\frac{dwdz}{w-z}\exp\left(n\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)-n\mathsf{D}_{1}(w)-n\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c})+n\mathsf{D}_{1}(z_{c})\right),

where the contours for
w
and
z
are as stated in Lemma
5.19
(Inner part).

We want to approximate
\mathsf{D}_{1}
and
\mathsf{D}_{2}
by
\overline{\mathsf{G}}
. For this purpose, we estimate
\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}-\overline{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}
. Compared to Lemma
5.10
, here we consider
z
much closer to
z_{c}
, thereby getting more refined estimates.
Take any
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z-z_{c}|\lesssim t_{c}^{3/2}
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)-\overline{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+1}^{-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-1}\frac{1}{nz+i}-\sum_{i=0}^{{\mathsf{t}}_{c}-1}\frac{1}{nz+i}

\displaystyle=\log(nz/(nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}))+\log((nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}+{\mathsf{t}}_{2})/(nz+{\mathsf{t}}_{c}))+\OO(n^{-1}t_{c}^{-1})

\displaystyle=\log\left(1+\frac{nz({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{c})+{\mathsf{x}}_{2}{\mathsf{t}}_{c}}{(nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2})(nz+{\mathsf{t}}_{c})}\right)+\OO(n^{-1}t_{c}^{-1}).

Using
\frac{z_{c}}{t_{c}}({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-nt_{c})+{\mathsf{x}}_{2}=n^{1/4}\gamma_{2}+\OO(1)\lesssim n^{1/4}
,
|z-z_{c}|\lesssim t_{c}^{3/2}
, and
z_{c},z_{c}+t_{c}\asymp t_{c}
, we obtain that

\frac{nz({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{c})+{\mathsf{x}}_{2}{\mathsf{t}}_{c}}{(nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2})(nz+{\mathsf{t}}_{c})}=\OO(n^{-3/4}t_{c}^{-1}).

With this estimate, we can derive that

(5.42)

\mathsf{D}^{\prime}_{2}(z)-\overline{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}(z)=\frac{nz({\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{c})+{\mathsf{x}}_{2}{\mathsf{t}}_{c}}{(nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2})(nz+{\mathsf{t}}_{c})}+\OO(n^{-1}t_{c}^{-1})=\frac{n^{1/2}(z-z_{c})\tau_{2}+n^{1/4}t_{c}\gamma_{2}}{(nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2})(z+t_{c})}+\OO(n^{-1}t_{c}^{-1}),

where we also used
{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{t}}_{c}=n^{1/2}\tau_{2}+\OO(1)
for the second equality.
Then, using
|z-z_{c}|\lesssim t_{c}^{3/2}
,
{\mathsf{x}}_{2}\lesssim n^{1/2}
, and
z_{c},z_{c}+t_{c}\asymp t_{c}
, we get that

\frac{n^{1/2}(z-z_{c})\tau_{2}+n^{1/4}t_{c}\gamma_{2}}{(nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2})(z+t_{c})}=\frac{n^{-1/2}(z-z_{c})\tau_{2}+n^{-3/4}t_{c}\gamma_{2}}{z_{c}(z_{c}+t_{c})}\left[1+\OO\left(|z-z_{c}|t_{c}^{-1}+n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-1}\right)\right].

If we further assume that
|z-z_{c}|\leq n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
, this expression reduces to

\frac{n^{1/2}(z-z_{c})\tau_{2}+n^{1/4}t_{c}\gamma_{2}}{(nz-{\mathsf{x}}_{2})(z+t_{c})}=-\frac{n^{-1/2}(z-z_{c})\tau_{2}}{t_{c}^{2}B(1-B)}-\frac{n^{-3/4}\gamma_{2}}{t_{c}B(1-B)}+\OO\left(n^{-3/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}^{-1}(n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}+n^{-1/2}t_{c}^{-1})\right).

Plugging this estimate into (
5.42
) and taking an integration over
z
, we obtain that

\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)-\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c})-\overline{\mathsf{G}}(z)+\overline{\mathsf{G}}(z_{c})+\frac{n^{-1/2}(z-z_{c})^{2}\tau_{2}}{2t_{c}^{2}B(1-B)}+\frac{n^{-3/4}(z-z_{c})\gamma_{2}}{t_{c}B(1-B)}\lesssim n^{-5/4+3\epsilon_{2}}+n^{-3/2+2\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}^{-1},

when
|z-z_{c}|\leq n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
.
By Lemma
5.9
, when
|z-z_{c}|\leq n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
, we have that

\overline{\mathsf{G}}(z)-\overline{\mathsf{G}}(z_{c})+t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{4}\lesssim n^{-5/4+5\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}^{-1/2}.

Then, using
n^{-1/2+\epsilon_{1}}<\overline{t}\asymp t_{c}
and the fact that
\epsilon_{2}
is small enough depending on
\epsilon_{1}
, we conclude that

\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)-\mathsf{D}(z_{c})+t_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{4}+\frac{n^{-1/2}(z-z_{c})^{2}\tau_{2}}{2t_{c}^{2}B(1-B)}+\frac{n^{-3/4}(z-z_{c})\gamma_{2}}{t_{c}B(1-B)}\lesssim n^{-1-\epsilon_{3}},

where
\epsilon_{3}>0
is a small enough constant depending on
\epsilon_{1}
and
\epsilon_{2}
. Similarly, we have

\mathsf{D}_{1}(w)-\mathsf{D}(z_{c})+t_{c}^{-4}A(w-z_{c})^{4}+\frac{n^{-1/2}(w-z_{c})^{2}\tau_{1}}{2t_{c}^{2}B(1-B)}+\frac{n^{-3/4}(w-z_{c})\gamma_{1}}{t_{c}B(1-B)}\lesssim n^{-1-\epsilon_{3}},

when
|w-z_{c}|\leq n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}
. Plugging the above two estimates into (
5.41
), we obtain

\frac{1}{t_{c}B(1-B)}\frac{1}{(2\pi\mathbf{i})^{2}}\iint\frac{dwdz}{w-z}\exp\Big(-nt_{c}^{-4}A(z-z_{c})^{4}+nt_{c}^{-4}A(w-z_{c})^{4}\\
-n^{1/2}\frac{\tau_{2}(z-z_{c})^{2}-\tau_{1}(w-z_{c})^{2}}{2t_{c}^{2}B(1-B)}-n^{1/4}\frac{\gamma_{2}(z-z_{c})-\gamma_{1}(w-z_{c})}{t_{c}B(1-B)}+\OO(n^{-\epsilon_{3}})\Big).

Introducing the rescaled variables
\mathbf{w}=\sqrt{2}(w-z_{c})n^{1/4}t_{c}^{-1}A^{1/4}
and
\mathbf{z}=\sqrt{2}(z-z_{c})n^{1/4}t_{c}^{-1}A^{1/4}
, we get (
5.35
), with the
\mathbf{w}
contour being

[\sqrt{2}e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{\epsilon_{2}}A^{1/4}\to 0\to\sqrt{2}e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{\epsilon_{2}}A^{1/4}]\cup[\sqrt{2}e^{5\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{\epsilon_{2}}A^{1/4}\to 0\to\sqrt{2}e^{7\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{\epsilon_{2}}A^{1/4}],

and the
\mathbf{z}
contour being

[\sqrt{2}e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/2}n^{\epsilon_{2}}A^{1/4}\to\sqrt{2}e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/2}n^{\epsilon_{2}}A^{1/4}].

We note that by replacing the
\mathbf{w}
and
\mathbf{z}
contours with (
5.36
), the integral in (
5.35
) changes by
\OO(\exp(-n^{4\epsilon_{2}}A/2))
, because the integrand along these contours is at most
\lesssim\exp((-\mathbf{z}^{4}+\mathbf{w}^{4})/8)
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

5.5.2.
Binomial and Gaussian

By classical CLT, it is expected that the binomial term in the NBRW kernel would lead to the Gaussian term in the Pearcey kernel.
We provide a detailed derivation here.

Lemma 5.21
.

If
\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}>n^{-\epsilon_{3}}
, then we have that

B^{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}(1-B)^{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}+{\mathsf{x}}_{2}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}}\binom{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}\\
=\frac{n^{-1/4}}{\sqrt{2\pi(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})B(1-B)}}\exp\left[-\frac{(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2})^{2}}{2B(1-B)(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})}\right]+\OO\left(n^{-1/2+3\epsilon_{3}}\right).

Proof.

Denote
{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}={\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}
and
{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}={\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}
. If
\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}>n^{-\epsilon_{3}}
, then we have
{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}={\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}-{\mathsf{x}}_{1}+{\mathsf{x}}_{2}>(1-B)n^{1/2-\epsilon_{3}}-Cn^{1/4}
and
{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}={\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}>Bn^{1/2-\epsilon_{3}}-Cn^{1/4}
. Then, by Stirling’s approximation, we have

\binom{{\mathsf{t}}_{1}-{\mathsf{t}}_{2}}{{\mathsf{x}}_{1}-{\mathsf{x}}_{2}}=\binom{{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}}{{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}}=\frac{{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}!}{{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}!({\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime})!}=\left[1+\OO(n^{-1/2+\epsilon_{3}})\right]\sqrt{\frac{{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}}{2\pi{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}({\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime})}}({\mathsf{t}}^{\prime})^{{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}}({\mathsf{x}}^{\prime})^{-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}}({\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime})^{-{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}+{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}}.

Using
{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}=(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})n^{1/2}+\OO(1)
and
{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}=B(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})n^{1/2}+(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2})n^{1/4}+\OO(1)
, we get that

(5.43)

\sqrt{\frac{{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}}{2\pi{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}({\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime})}}=\frac{n^{-1/4}(1+\OO(n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{3}}))}{\sqrt{2\pi(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})B(1-B)}},

and

\displaystyle\log\Big(B^{{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}}(1-B)^{{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}}({\mathsf{t}}^{\prime})^{{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}}({\mathsf{x}}^{\prime})^{-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}}({\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime})^{-{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}+{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}}\Big)={\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}\log\frac{B{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}}{{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}}+({\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime})\log\frac{(1-B){\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}}{{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[B(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})n^{1/2}+(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2})n^{1/4}\right]\log\left(1+B^{-1}\frac{\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}}{\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}}n^{-1/4}\right)

(5.44)

\displaystyle-\left[(1-B)(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})n^{1/2}-(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2})n^{1/4}\right]\log\left(1-(1-B)^{-1}\frac{\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}}{\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}}n^{-1/4}\right)+\OO(n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{3}}),

where for the second estimate, we used that the derivatives of the left-hand side with respect to
{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}
are

\log\frac{B}{1-B}-\log\frac{{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}}{{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}}\lesssim n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{3}},\quad\log(1-B)-\log\frac{{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}-{\mathsf{x}}^{\prime}}{{\mathsf{t}}^{\prime}}\lesssim n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{3}}.

Taking the Taylor expansion of the logarithms in (
5.44
), we get

\eqref{eq:xpr}=-\frac{(\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2})^{2}}{2B(1-B)(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})}+\OO(n^{-1/4+3\epsilon_{3}}).

Multiplying the exponential of this estimate with the right-hand side of (
5.43
) concludes the proof.
∎

5.6.
Smallness of outer contour integral

It remains to prove the outer part of Lemma
5.19
.
By Lemma
5.20
, it suffices to show that the following integral

(5.45)

\iint\frac{dwdz}{w-z}\exp\left(n\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)-n\mathsf{D}_{1}(w)-n\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c})+n\mathsf{D}_{1}(z_{c})\right)

over the contours stated in the outer part of Lemma
5.19
is bounded by
\OO(\exp(-cn^{4\epsilon_{2}}))
.

We recall from Section
5.4.1
the curves
\ell_{2,1}
,
\ell_{2,2}
,
\ell_{2,3}
on which
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{2})=0
, and the curves
\ell_{1,1}
,
\ell_{1,2}
,
\ell_{1,3}
on which
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{1})=0
.
We will deform the
w
contours

[z_{c}+e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to-t_{c}-1\to z_{c}+e^{5\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}]
and
[z_{c}+e^{7\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to 1\to z_{c}+e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/4}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}]

to curves that closely follow
\ell_{1,1}
,
\ell_{1,3}
and their complex conjugates (denoted by
\overline{\ell}_{1,1}
and
\overline{\ell}_{1,3}
). We will deform the
z
contours
[\infty e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/2}\to z_{c}+e^{3\pi\mathbf{i}/2}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}]
and
[z_{c}+e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/2}n^{-1/4+\epsilon_{2}}t_{c}\to\infty e^{\pi\mathbf{i}/2}]
to curves that closely follow
\ell_{2,2}
and its complex conjugate (denoted by
\overline{\ell}_{2,2}
).
Along these curves,
n(\mathsf{D}_{2}(z)-\mathsf{D}_{2}(z_{c}))
and
-n(\mathsf{D}_{1}(w)-\mathsf{D}_{1}(z_{c}))
are (almost) real and negative, and of order at least
\gtrsim n^{4\epsilon_{2}}
by Lemma
5.17
.

As indicated earlier, some issues may appear as we lose precise control of the behaviors of these curves.
First, it is unclear whether
\ell_{1,1}
and
\ell_{1,3}
are disjoint from
\ell_{2,2}
, so we need to consider the residues resulting from their possible intersections.
Second, it could be technical to control the length of these curves. We will circumvent this issue by discretizing these curves.

5.6.1.
Intersections

For possible intersections between the curves
\ell_{1,1}
,
\ell_{1,3}
, and
\ell_{2,2}
, we consider the zero set of
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}(\mathsf{D}_{1}-\mathsf{D}_{2})
