\widetilde{z}_{c}-z_{c}=\widetilde{x}_{c}-\widetilde{t}_{c}\widetilde{w}_{\widetilde{t}_{c}}(\widetilde{x}_{c})=\OO(\Delta t^{2})
(recall that
z_{c}=0
) and

\widetilde{w}_{0}(\widetilde{z}_{c})-{\mathfrak{r}}^{-1}=\widetilde{w}_{0}(\widetilde{x}_{c}-\widetilde{t}_{c}\widetilde{w}_{\widetilde{t}_{c}}(x_{c}))-w_{t_{c}}(x_{c})=\OO(\Delta t+|\widetilde{x}_{c}-\widetilde{t}_{c}\widetilde{w}_{\widetilde{t}_{c}}(\widetilde{x}_{c})|)=\OO(\Delta t).

With these two estimates and the estimate on
\widetilde{t}_{c}
, we finally get that

\widetilde{x}_{c}=\widetilde{t}_{c}\widetilde{w}_{\widetilde{t}_{c}}(\widetilde{x}_{c})+\OO(\Delta t^{2})=(t_{c}+\OO(\Delta t^{2}))({\mathfrak{r}}^{-1}+\OO(\Delta t))+\OO(\Delta t^{2})=x_{c}+\OO(\Delta t^{2}).

7.5.2.
Proof of
4.4

We can define the NBRW height function as

(7.58)

\widetilde{H}(x,t)=-M/n+\int_{-\infty}^{x}\widetilde{\rho}_{t}(y){\rm d}y.

Then, for
t\in[0,t_{1}]
and
i\in[\![{-M,N}]\!]
, we define
\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(t)
as in (
4.1
) with
H^{*}
replaced by
\widetilde{H}
. Similar to (
2.7
), the complex slope
\widetilde{f}_{t}
is related to the height function
\widetilde{H}(x,t)
through

(7.59)

\arg^{*}\widetilde{f}_{t}(x)=-\pi\partial_{x}\widetilde{H}(x,t),\qquad\arg^{*}\left(\widetilde{f}_{t}(x)+1\right)=\pi\partial_{t}\widetilde{H}(x,t).

Proof of (
7.59
).

The first equation follows directly from the definition of
\widetilde{H}
and (
7.42
).
The second equation can be derived by

\displaystyle\pi\partial_{t}\widetilde{H}(x,t)

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{Im}\,\int_{-\infty}^{x}\partial_{t}\widetilde{m}_{t}(y){\rm d}y=-\mathrm{Im}\,\int_{-\infty}^{x}\partial_{t}\log\widetilde{f}_{t}(y){\rm d}y=\mathrm{Im}\,\int_{-\infty}^{x}\partial_{y}\log(\widetilde{f}_{t}(y)+1){\rm d}y

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Im}\,\log(\widetilde{f}_{t}(x)+1)=\pi\arg^{*}(\widetilde{f}_{t}(x)+1),

where we used the complex Burgers equation (
7.43
), rewritten as

\partial_{t}\log\widetilde{f}_{t}(z)+\partial_{z}\log(\widetilde{f}_{t}(z)+1)=0
.
∎

We need the following optimal rigidity estimate, for the NBRW
\widetilde{\mathsf{Q}}=\{\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}_{i}\}_{i=-M}^{N}:[\![{0,\infty}]\!]\to\mathbb{Z}^{[\![{-M,N}]\!]}
constructed in
Section

4.2
.
It follows from
4.2
and
[
Hua21
, Proposition 4.4]
(which has been stated as
6.4
in the tiling setting).

Lemma 7.8
.

Under the setting of
4.4
, with overwhelming probability:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}_{L}(nt)/n-\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t),\ \widetilde{\mathsf{q}}_{-L}(nt)/n-\widetilde{\gamma}_{-L}(t)\lesssim n^{-3/4-{\mathfrak{d}}},

\displaystyle\quad\forall t\in[0,t_{1}]\cap n^{-1}\mathbb{Z},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathsf{q}}_{i}(nt_{1})/n-\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(t_{1})\lesssim n^{-3/4-{\mathfrak{d}}},

\displaystyle\quad\forall i\in[\![{-L,L}]\!].

Now, to conclude
4.4
, it remains to show that the quantiles
\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}
are sufficiently close to
\gamma_{i}
, which is the content of the following lemma. It will be proved in the next subsection.

Lemma 7.9
.

Under the setting of
4.4
, we have

(7.60)

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)-\gamma_{L}(t)|+|\widetilde{\gamma}_{-L}(t)-\gamma_{-L}(t)|\leq n^{\delta}\Delta t^{5/2},

\displaystyle\quad\forall t\in[0,t_{1}],

(7.61)

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(t_{1})-\gamma_{i}(t_{1})|\leq n^{\delta}\Delta t^{2},

\displaystyle\quad\forall i\in[\![{-L,L}]\!].

7.6.
Evolution of quantiles: Proofs of
7.2
and
7.9

We first define functions

h_{t}(z):=z+tw_{0}(z),\quad\widetilde{h}_{t}(z):=z+t\widetilde{w}_{0}(z)=z+t(w_{0}(z)+\Delta w_{0}(z)).

Then, (
7.14
) and a similar equation for
\widetilde{w}_{t}
from (
7.43
) give that, for
t\in[0,t_{1}]
and
\xi\in\mathscr{U}_{t}
,

(7.62)

w_{t}(\xi)=w_{0}(h_{t}^{-1}(\xi)),\quad\widetilde{w}_{t}(\xi)=\widetilde{w}_{0}(\widetilde{h}_{t}^{-1}(\xi)).

Using (
7.50
), we get that

\displaystyle w_{t}(\xi)-\widetilde{w}_{t}(\xi)

\displaystyle=w_{0}(h_{t}^{-1}(\xi))-w_{0}(\widetilde{h}_{t}^{-1}(\xi))-\Delta w_{0}(\widetilde{h}_{t}^{-1}(\xi))

\displaystyle=w_{0}(u_{t})-w_{0}({\widetilde{u}_{t}})-\Delta w_{0}({\widetilde{u}_{t}})=\varpi_{t}(\xi)-\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\xi)+\OO(|{\widetilde{u}_{t}}(\xi)|),

where we abbreviated that

u_{t}=u_{t}(\xi)=h_{t}^{-1}(\xi),\;{\widetilde{u}_{t}}={\widetilde{u}_{t}}(\xi)=\widetilde{h}_{t}^{-1}(\xi),\;\varpi_{t}=\varpi_{t}(\xi)=w_{0}(u_{t}(\xi))-{\mathfrak{r}}^{-1},\;\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}=\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\xi)=w_{0}({\widetilde{u}_{t}}(\xi))-{\mathfrak{r}}^{-1}.

These variables satisfy the following equations:

(7.63)

\displaystyle u_{t}+t\varpi_{t}=\xi-{\mathfrak{r}}^{-1}t={\widetilde{u}_{t}}+t(\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}+\Delta w_{0}({\widetilde{u}_{t}})),

(7.64)

\displaystyle u_{t}=F_{0}(\varpi_{t})=-t_{c}\varpi_{t}+\frac{a}{3}\varpi_{t}^{3}+\cal E(\varpi_{t}),

(7.65)

\displaystyle{\widetilde{u}_{t}}=F_{0}(\widetilde{\varpi}_{t})=-t_{c}\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}+\frac{a}{3}\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}^{3}+\cal E(\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}),

where (
7.64
) and (
7.65
) are by (
7.55
).

From
{\rm d}H^{*}(\gamma_{i}(t),t)/{\rm d}t=0
, using (
2.7
), we can derive that

(7.66)

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}^{\prime}(t)=\frac{\arg^{*}[f_{t}(\gamma_{i}(t))+1]}{\arg^{*}[f_{t}(\gamma_{i}(t))]}.

A similar differential equation for
\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(t)
with
f_{t}
replaced by
\widetilde{f}_{t}
can also be derived using (
7.59
).
Since
f_{t_{c}}(x_{c})=({\mathfrak{r}}-1)^{-1}
is a positive constant, for
z=f_{t_{c}}(x_{c})+\oo(1)
, we have that

\frac{\arg^{*}(z+1)}{\arg^{*}(z)}=\frac{\arctan\frac{\mathrm{Im}\,z}{f_{t_{c}}(x_{c})+1+\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(z-f_{t_{c}}(x_{c}))}}{\arctan\frac{\mathrm{Im}\,z}{f_{t_{c}}(x_{c})+\mathop{\mathrm{Re}}(z-f_{t_{c}}(x_{c}))}}.

Hence, with the Taylor expansion of
\arctan
, we deduce that

(7.67)

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}^{\prime}(t)={\mathfrak{r}}^{-1}+\OO(|w_{t}(\gamma_{i}(t))-w_{t_{c}}(x_{c})|),\quad\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}^{\prime}(t)={\mathfrak{r}}^{-1}+\OO(|\widetilde{w}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(t))-w_{t_{c}}(x_{c})|),

(7.68)

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}^{\prime}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}^{\prime}(t)\lesssim f_{t}(\gamma_{i}(t))-\widetilde{f}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(t))\lesssim w_{t}(\gamma_{i}(t))-\widetilde{w}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(t)).

We next complete the proofs of
7.2
and
7.9
using (
7.63
)–(
7.68
).

7.6.1.
Proof of
7.2

At
t=t_{c}
, we have
\gamma_{0}(t_{c})=x_{c}
. Then, from (
7.63
) and (
7.64
), we obtain that

(t-t_{c})\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{0}(t))+\frac{a}{3}\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{0}(t))^{3}+\cal E(\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{0}(t)))=\gamma_{0}(t)-c(t)=\int_{t_{c}}^{t}(\gamma_{0}^{\prime}(t^{\prime})-{\mathfrak{r}}^{-1}){\rm d}t^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{t_{c}}^{t}|\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{0}(t^{\prime}))|{\rm d}t^{\prime},

where we used the fact that
t/{\mathfrak{r}}=c(t)
(since
x_{c}=t_{c}/{\mathfrak{r}}
) and applied (
7.67
) for the last inequality.
We can rewrite the above equation as (
7.38
), with
\varpi=\varpi(t)=\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{0}(t))
,
|\varepsilon(\varpi)|\leq C_{1}|\varpi|^{4}
,
\tau=(t-t_{c})/a
, and

y(\varpi):=\frac{3}{2a}\int_{t_{c}}^{t}\gamma_{0}^{\prime}(t^{\prime})-{\mathfrak{r}}^{-1}{\rm d}t^{\prime},\quad|y(\varpi)|\leq C_{2}\int_{t_{c}}^{t}|\varpi(t^{\prime})|{\rm d}t^{\prime},

for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
. Then, we have
\varpi=q_{t}(\varpi)
, where the function
q_{t}(\varpi)
is defined in terms of
\tau
,
\varepsilon(\varpi)
, and
y(\varpi)
as the right-hand side of (
7.39
). Thus,

|q_{t}(\varpi)|\leq 4|y(\varpi)|^{1/3}+4|\varepsilon(\varpi)|^{1/3}+2\tau^{1/2}\leq 4C_{2}^{1/3}|t-t_{c}|^{1/3}\sup_{t^{\prime}\in[t_{c},t]}|\varpi(t^{\prime})|^{1/3}+4C_{1}^{1/3}|\varpi(t)|^{4/3}+2\sqrt{\frac{t-t_{c}}{a}}.

With this bound, we can check that there exists a constant
\cal A>0
depending on
a,C_{1},C_{2}
such that if
|\varpi(t^{\prime})|\leq\cal A|t-t_{c}|^{1/2}
for all
t^{\prime}\in[t_{c},t]
, then we have
|\varpi(t)|\leq\cal A|t-t_{c}|^{1/2}/2
.

Combining the above fact with the continuity of
\varpi(t)
, we can conclude that
|\varpi(t)|\leq\cal A|t-t_{c}|^{1/2}/2
for all
t\in[t_{c},t_{1}]
. More precisely, we first notice that
\varpi(t)
is Hölder-
1/3
continuous in
t
(since the right-hand side of (
7.39
) is Hölder-
1/3
continuous in both
t
and
x
and
\gamma_{0}(t)
is Lipschitz continuous in
t
). Suppose
|\varpi(t^{\prime})|\leq\cal A|t^{\prime}-t_{c}|^{1/2}/2\leq\cal A|t-t_{c}|^{1/2}/2
for all
t^{\prime}\in[t_{c},t]
. Then, for a sufficiently small
\varepsilon
,
|\varpi(t^{\prime})|\leq\cal A|t^{\prime}-t_{c}|^{1/2}
for all
t^{\prime}\in[t_{c},t+\varepsilon]
, from which we get that
|\varpi(t^{\prime})|=|q_{t^{\prime}}(\varpi)|\leq\cal A|t^{\prime}-t_{c}|^{1/2}/2
for all
t^{\prime}\in[t_{c},t+\varepsilon]
. In this way, we can extend the estimate
|\varpi(t)|\leq\cal A|t-t_{c}|^{1/2}/2
at
t=t_{c}
all the way to
t_{1}
.

Finally, plugging the estimate
|\varpi(t)|\leq\cal A|t-t_{c}|^{1/2}/2
into (
7.64
), we conclude the proof.

7.6.2.
Proof of (
7.60
)

To bound
|\gamma_{L}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)|
, we now bound the right-hand side of (
7.68
) for
i=L
.

By
4.1
and (
7.23
) (recall that
x_{c}=t_{c}/{\mathfrak{r}}
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(0)=\gamma_{i}(0)
for
i\in\llbracket-M,N\rrbracket
), we have

(7.69)

\gamma_{L}(0)=\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(0)\asymp n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{3/2}.

For
t\in[0,t_{1}]
, we get from (
7.63
)–(
7.65
) (and using (
7.50
)) that

(7.70)

\displaystyle\gamma_{L}(t)-t/{\mathfrak{r}}

\displaystyle=F_{0}(\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t)))+t\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))=(t-t_{c})\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))+\frac{a}{3}\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))^{3}+\cal E(\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))),

(7.71)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)-t/{\mathfrak{r}}

\displaystyle=F_{0}(\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)))+t(\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))+\Delta w_{0}({\widetilde{u}_{t}}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))))

\displaystyle=(t-t_{c})\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))+\frac{a}{3}\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))^{3}+\cal E(\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)))+\OO(|{\widetilde{u}_{t}}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))|\Delta t).

We first assume the following a priori bound:

(7.72)

|\gamma_{L}(t)-t/{\mathfrak{r}}|\leq n^{\delta}\Delta t^{3/2},\quad|\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)-t/{\mathfrak{r}}|\leq n^{\delta}\Delta t^{3/2},\quad\forall t\in[0,t_{1}].

Under (
7.72
), using (
7.70
) and (
7.71
), we can check that for any
t\in[0,t_{1}]
,

\begin{split}\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))=\OO(n^{\delta/3}\Delta t^{1/2}),\quad&u_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))=F_{0}(w(\gamma_{L}(t)))=\OO(n^{\delta}\Delta t^{3/2}),\\
\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))=\OO(n^{\delta/3}\Delta t^{1/2}),\quad&{\widetilde{u}_{t}}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))=F_{0}(\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)))=\OO(n^{\delta}\Delta t^{3/2}),\end{split}

which imply that

(7.73)

\begin{split}&|w_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))-w_{t_{c}}(x_{c})|=|\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))|=\OO(n^{\delta/3}\Delta t^{1/2}),\\
&|\widetilde{w}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))-w_{t_{c}}(x_{c})|=|\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))|+\OO(|{\widetilde{u}_{t}}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))|)=\OO(n^{\delta/3}\Delta t^{1/2}).\end{split}

Plugging these estimates into (
7.67
) for
i=L
yields that for
t\in[0,t_{1}]
,

(7.74)

\displaystyle\gamma_{L}^{\prime}(t)={\mathfrak{r}}^{-1}+\OO(n^{\delta/3}\Delta t^{1/2}),\quad\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}^{\prime}(t)={\mathfrak{r}}^{-1}+\OO(n^{\delta/3}\Delta t^{1/2}).

By integrating them, we obtain that

(7.75)

\gamma_{L}(t)-\gamma_{L}(0)-t/{\mathfrak{r}}=\OO(n^{\delta/3}\Delta t^{3/2}),\quad\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)-\gamma_{L}(0)-t/{\mathfrak{r}}=\OO(n^{\delta/3}\Delta t^{3/2}),

under (
7.72
). Note that (
7.69
) and (
7.75
) together imply (
7.72
). Thus, to show (
7.75
) without assuming (
7.72
), we only need to consider an
n^{-10}t_{1}
-net of
[0,t_{1}]
and use a simple induction argument. More precisely, we define a sequence of times
{\mathfrak{t}}_{k}:=kn^{-10}t_{1}
,
k=0,1,\ldots,n^{10}
. First, the estimates (
7.72
) and (
7.75
) hold at
t={\mathfrak{t}}_{0}
by (
7.69
). Second, suppose (
7.75
) holds at some
{\mathfrak{t}}_{k}
. With (
7.63
)–(
7.65
), we can check that
\gamma_{L}(t)
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)
are Hölder-
1/3
continuous in
t
. Thus, from (
7.69
) and (
7.75
) at
t={\mathfrak{t}}_{k}
, we obtain that (
7.72
) holds uniformly for all
t\in[{\mathfrak{t}}_{k},{\mathfrak{t}}_{k+1}]
. The arguments above then imply that (
7.75
) holds at
t={\mathfrak{t}}_{k+1}
. With mathematical induction in
k
, we conclude (
7.75
) for all
t\in[0,t_{1}]
.

Now, with (
7.69
) and (
7.75
), we get that

(7.76)

\gamma_{L}(t)-t/{\mathfrak{r}}=(1+\oo(1))(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)-t/{\mathfrak{r}})=(1+\oo(1))\gamma_{L}(0)\asymp n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{3/2}.

Applying it to equations (
7.70
) and (
7.71
), we obtain that

(7.77)

\begin{split}&\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))=(1+\oo(1))\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))\asymp n^{\delta/4}\Delta t^{1/2},\\
&u_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))=(1+\oo(1)){\widetilde{u}_{t}}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))=(1+\oo(1))\gamma_{L}(0)\asymp n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{3/2}.\end{split}

Subtracting the equation (
7.70
) from (
7.71
) and applying (
7.77
) yield that

\displaystyle|\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))-\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))|\cdot|\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))|^{2}\lesssim\frac{a}{3}|\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))^{3}-\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))^{3}|\leq|\gamma_{L}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)|

\displaystyle\quad+|t-t_{c}||\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))-\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))|+|\cal E(\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t)))-\cal E(\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)))|+\OO(|{\widetilde{u}_{t}}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))|\Delta t)

(7.78)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\gamma_{L}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)|+(\Delta t+|\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))|^{3})|\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))-\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))|+n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{5/2}.

Thus, we obtain that

\displaystyle|\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))-\widetilde{\varpi}_{t}(\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t))|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{5/2}+|\gamma_{L}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)|}{|\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))|^{2}-\OO(\Delta t+|\varpi_{t}(\gamma_{L}(t))|^{3})}

(7.79)

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{\delta/4}\Delta t^{3/2}+n^{-\delta/2}\Delta t^{-1}|\gamma_{L}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)|,

which, together with (
7.68
) for
i=L
, implies that

\displaystyle|\gamma_{L}^{\prime}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}^{\prime}(t)|\lesssim n^{\delta/4}\Delta t^{3/2}+n^{-\delta/2}\Delta t^{-1}|\gamma_{L}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)|.

Finally, an application of the Grönwall’s inequality gives that

\max_{0\leq t\leq t_{1}}|\gamma_{L}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t)|\leq n^{\delta/2}\Delta t^{5/2}.

The proof for the bound on
|\gamma_{-L}(t)-\widetilde{\gamma}_{-L}(t)|
is similar.

7.6.3.
Proof of (
7.61
)

To be concise, we abuse the notations and abbreviate
u(\xi):=u_{t_{1}}(\xi)
,
\varpi(\xi):=\varpi_{t_{1}}(\xi)
and
\widetilde{u}(\xi):=\widetilde{u}_{t_{1}}(\xi)
,
\widetilde{\varpi}(\xi):=\widetilde{\varpi}_{t_{1}}(\xi)
.
By (
7.63
)–(
7.65
) and (
7.50
), for any fixed
\xi\in[\gamma_{-L}(t_{1})\wedge\widetilde{\gamma}_{-L}(t_{1}),\gamma_{L}(t_{1})\vee\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t_{1})]
,
\varpi(\xi)
and
\widetilde{\varpi}(\xi)
satisfy the equations

(7.80)

\displaystyle(t_{1}-t_{c})\varpi+\frac{a}{3}\varpi^{3}+\cal E(\varpi)=\xi-t_{1}/{\mathfrak{r}},

(7.81)

\displaystyle(t_{1}-t_{c})\widetilde{\varpi}+\frac{a}{3}\widetilde{\varpi}^{3}+\cal E(\widetilde{\varpi})=\xi-t_{1}/{\mathfrak{r}}+\OO(\Delta t|F_{0}(\widetilde{\varpi})|).

By equation (
7.76
), the chosen
\xi
satisfies
|\xi-t_{1}/{\mathfrak{r}}|\lesssim n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{3/2}
. Then, from (
7.80
), (
7.81
) and (
7.64
), (
7.65
), we obtain that

(7.82)

|\varpi(\xi)|+|\widetilde{\varpi}(\xi)|\lesssim n^{\delta/4}\Delta t^{1/2},\quad|u(\xi)|+|\widetilde{u}(\xi)|\lesssim n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{3/2}.

First, consider the case where
|\xi-t_{1}/{\mathfrak{r}}|>C\Delta t^{3/2}
for a large enough constant
C>0
, so that the
\frac{a}{3}\varpi^{3}
and
\frac{a}{3}\widetilde{\varpi}^{3}
terms dominate in (
7.80
) and (
7.81
). In particular, we can choose
C
such that

(7.83)

\varpi\asymp\widetilde{\varpi}\asymp|\xi-t_{1}/{\mathfrak{r}}|^{1/3},\quad\frac{a}{3}|\varpi^{3}-\widetilde{\varpi}^{3}|>2(t_{1}-t_{c})|\varpi-\widetilde{\varpi}|.

Subtracting the equations (
7.80
) and (
7.81
) and using a similar argument as in (
7.78
) and (
7.79
), we get

(7.84)

|\varpi-\widetilde{\varpi}|\lesssim\frac{\Delta t|F_{0}(\widetilde{\varpi})|}{|\varpi|^{2}-\OO(|\varpi|^{3})}\lesssim\frac{\Delta t|\widetilde{\varpi}|^{3}+\Delta t^{2}|\widetilde{\varpi}|}{|\varpi|^{2}}\lesssim n^{\delta/4}\Delta t^{3/2},

where we used (
7.82
) and (
7.83
) in the last step.

We next consider the case where
|\xi-t_{1}/{\mathfrak{r}}|\leq C\Delta t^{3/2}
. From (
7.80
) and (
7.81
), we get

(7.85)

|\varpi|\lesssim\Delta t^{1/2},\quad|\widetilde{\varpi}|\lesssim\Delta t^{1/2}.

Moreover, we can write (
7.80
) and (
7.81
) as

\displaystyle\varpi^{3}+3\tau\varpi-2(y+\varepsilon_{1})=0,\quad\widetilde{\varpi}^{3}+3\tau\widetilde{\varpi}-2(y+\varepsilon_{2})=0,

where
\tau:=(t_{1}-t_{c})/a
,
y:=3(\xi-t_{1}/{\mathfrak{r}})/(2a)
,
\varepsilon_{1}=\OO(|\varpi|^{4})
and
\varepsilon_{2}=\OO(|\widetilde{\varpi}|^{4}+\Delta t|F_{0}(\widetilde{\varpi})|)
. Using the general cubic formula, we obtain that

(7.86)

\begin{split}&\varpi=\alpha\left[(y+\varepsilon_{1})+\sqrt{(y+\varepsilon_{1})^{2}+\tau^{3}}\right]^{1/3}+\alpha^{-1}\left[(y+\varepsilon_{1})-\sqrt{(y+\varepsilon_{1})^{2}+\tau^{3}}\right]^{1/3},\\
&\widetilde{\varpi}=\alpha\left[(y+\varepsilon_{2})+\sqrt{(y+\varepsilon_{2})^{2}+\tau^{3}}\right]^{1/3}+\alpha^{-1}\left[(y+\varepsilon_{2})-\sqrt{(y+\varepsilon_{2})^{2}+\tau^{3}}\right]^{1/3},\end{split}

where
\alpha
is a primitive cube root of unity chosen such that
\mathrm{Im}\,\varpi<0
and
\mathrm{Im}\,\widetilde{\varpi}<0
. With the estimates
\tau\gtrsim\Delta t
,
|y|\lesssim\Delta t^{3/2}
, and
\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}=\OO(\Delta t^{2})
by (
7.85
), it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\left|\left[(y+\varepsilon_{1})\pm\sqrt{(y+\varepsilon_{1})^{2}+\tau^{3}}\right]^{1/3}-\left[(y+\varepsilon_{2})\pm\sqrt{(y+\varepsilon_{2})^{2}+\tau^{3}}\right]^{1/3}\right|\lesssim\frac{|\varepsilon_{1}|+|\varepsilon_{2}|}{\tau}\lesssim\Delta t,

thereby giving that
|\varpi-\widetilde{\varpi}|\lesssim\Delta t
.
Combining this and (
7.84
), we obtain that for
\xi\in[\gamma_{-L}(t_{1})\wedge\widetilde{\gamma}_{-L}(t_{1}),\gamma_{L}(t_{1})\vee\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t_{1})]
,

(7.87)

|f_{t_{1}}(\xi)-\widetilde{f}_{t_{1}}(\xi)|\lesssim|w_{t_{1}}(\xi)-\widetilde{w}_{t_{1}}(\xi)|\lesssim|\varpi-\widetilde{\varpi}|+\OO(|\widetilde{z}|)\lesssim\Delta t,

where we also used
n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{1/2}\ll 1
since
0<\delta<\omega/2
.
Since
f_{t_{1}}
and
\widetilde{f}_{t_{1}}
are bounded away from
0
, we have

(7.88)

|\rho_{t_{1}}(\xi)-\widetilde{\rho}_{t_{1}}(\xi)|=\frac{1}{\pi}\left|\arg^{*}f_{t_{1}}(\xi)-\arg^{*}\widetilde{f}_{t_{1}}(\xi)\right|\lesssim|f_{t_{1}}(\xi)-\widetilde{f}_{t_{1}}(\xi)|\lesssim\Delta t.

On the other hand, by Item 2 of
7.1
(whose proof also applies to
\widetilde{\rho}_{t_{1}}
), we have

(7.89)

\rho_{t_{1}}(\xi)\asymp\widetilde{\rho}_{t_{1}}(\xi)\asymp\begin{cases}\Delta t^{1/2},&|\xi-{t_{1}}/{\mathfrak{r}}|\leq C\Delta t^{3/2}\\
|\xi-{t_{1}}/{\mathfrak{r}}|^{1/3},&C\Delta t^{3/2}\leq|\xi-{t_{1}}/{\mathfrak{r}}|\leq Cn^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{3/2}\end{cases}.

Now, we compare the quantiles
\gamma_{i}({t_{1}})
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}({t_{1}})
through the following equation:

(7.90)

\int_{\gamma_{i}({t_{1}})}^{\gamma_{L}({t_{1}})}\rho_{t_{1}}(\xi){\rm d}\xi=\frac{L-i}{n}=\int_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}({t_{1}})}^{\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}({t_{1}})}\widetilde{\rho}_{t_{1}}(\xi){\rm d}\xi,\quad i\in[\![{-L,L}]\!].

With (
7.88
), we can write

\displaystyle\int_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}({t_{1}})}^{\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}({t_{1}})}\widetilde{\rho}_{t_{1}}(\xi){\rm d}\xi

\displaystyle=\int_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}({t_{1}})}^{\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}({t_{1}})}\rho_{t_{1}}(\xi){\rm d}\xi+\OO\left(\Delta t|\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}({t_{1}})-\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}({t_{1}})|\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}({t_{1}})}^{\gamma_{L}({t_{1}})}\rho_{t_{1}}(\xi){\rm d}\xi+\OO\left(n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{5/2}+n^{\delta/4}\Delta t^{1/2}\left|\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}({t_{1}})-\gamma_{L}({t_{1}})\right|\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}({t_{1}})}^{\gamma_{L}({t_{1}})}\rho_{t_{1}}(\xi){\rm d}\xi+\OO(n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{5/2}),

where in the second step we used (
7.89
) and the estimates
|\xi-t_{1}/{\mathfrak{r}}|\lesssim n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{3/2}
,
|\widetilde{\gamma}_{L}(t_{1})-\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}(t_{1})|\lesssim n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{3/2}
by (
7.76
), and in the third step we used (
7.60
).
Plugging it into (
7.90
) then gives

\int_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}({t_{1}})\wedge\gamma_{i}({t_{1}})}^{\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}({t_{1}})\vee\gamma_{i}({t_{1}})}\rho_{t_{1}}(\xi){\rm d}\xi\lesssim n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{5/2}.

Combining this equation with (
7.89
), we get that

\Delta t^{1/2}|\widetilde{\gamma}_{i}({t_{1}})-\gamma_{i}({t_{1}})|\lesssim n^{3\delta/4}\Delta t^{5/2}
,
which concludes (
7.61
).
